# FIXES Archive

Historical archive of completed FIX-NNN items, moved out of `docs/FIXES.md` by `pnpm fixes:archive`. Append-only — newest block on top.

## Archived 2026-05-31

### BUGS — Fix These First

- [x] 🔴 L — **Graph presets ignore focused entity — Ted Cruz treemap shows disjoint PAC sets across "By State" and "PAC Money by Sector"** — `/api/graph/treemap-pac` accepts no `entityId` and returns ALL PACs globally, while `/api/graph/treemap` is entity-aware. Same toolbar, two scopes. Root cause: presets carry no entity-type metadata; `GraphConfigPanel.tsx:827-841` filter only checks `vizType`. Fix: (a) extend `GraphViewPreset.meta` with `applicableEntityTypes`, `dataModeByEntity`, `intent`; (b) add `resolvePresetForFocus` helper that runs in `applyPreset` and re-runs on focus change unless `meta.isDirty`; (c) add `entityId` param to `/api/graph/treemap-pac` (pre-filter PAC set to donors of that official); (d) fix three drifted presets per `packages/graph/CLAUDE.md` Built-in Presets table — `COMMITTEE_POWER` add `appointment`, `INDUSTRY_CAPTURE` add `oversight,revolving_door`, `CO_SPONSOR_NETWORK` add `vote_yes`; (e) `GraphConfigPanel` renders presets in two buckets — "Native" + "Adapted for {focusName}". <!--id:FIX-216-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Treemap aggregate mode shows $0 for everyone except one official (PostgREST 1000-row truncation)** — Senate Democrats > Treemap > Group by State showed Maria Cantwell at $1.9M and 44 other senators at $0. Per-batch financial_relationships scan returned at most 1000 rows; Cantwell's rows happened to be in the first 1000 and the rest dropped. Fix: read `officials.total_received_cents` (denormalized May 8 column) directly in default aggregate mode; use `range()` pagination on industry_filter path and on the connection-count / vote-count aggregations. <!--id:FIX-219-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Donation floor as a user option (right panel slider)** — Prior to this fix `treemap-pac` party mode hardcoded `> $10k` and `treemap` ignored `donation.minAmount` entirely. Force respected the buried Min $ field in Connections row but no other viz did. Added `DonationFloorControl` log-scale slider ($0 / $200 / $1k / $10k / $100k / $1M stops) to TreemapSettings + ForceSettings; binds to `view.connections.donation.minAmount`. Wired both `/api/graph/treemap` (entity + aggregate mode) and `/api/graph/treemap-pac` (sector + party mode) to honor `minAmountUsd` query param. Replaces the hardcoded $10k cutoff. <!--id:FIX-220-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Materialize the three global-aggregate chord RPCs** — `chord_donor_type_party_flows()`, `chord_donor_state_party_flows()`, `chord_subject_party_flows()` (all introduced in FIX-221) full-scanned `financial_relationships` (~2.2M rows on prod) and consistently exceeded the 5s `withDbTimeout` ceiling, returning empty groups for the global mode of each chord. Same pattern as FIX-207 (`chord_industry_flows_mv`): pre-aggregate nightly into `chord_donor_type_party_flows_mv`, `chord_donor_state_party_flows_mv`, `chord_subject_party_flows_mv`; replace each function body with `SELECT FROM mv WHERE param IS NULL UNION ALL <cohort path>` so the MV serves the global case and the live aggregation handles per-cohort calls. Three new `refresh_*` helpers wired into `runNightlySync()` step 7 alongside the existing chord MV refresh. <!--id:FIX-222-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Donor profile total missing super-PAC contributions — `total_donated_cents` doesn't include donor → committee outflow** — Musk's `/donors/[id]` page shows ~$941k headline but live SUM of his outflow in `financial_relationships` is ~$277M (chiefly America PAC contributions added by FIX-236). Two compounding drifts: (1) FEC indiv writer upserts with `onConflict: 'donor_fingerprint'` which overwrites `total_donated_cents` each cycle, so multi-cycle donors carry only the last-cycle slice; (2) FIX-236 committee rows were added retroactively without a re-run, so even single-cycle donors miss them. Page secondary aggregations (Top recipients, recipient count) already live-sum from `financial_relationships` — only the headline drifts. Fix: new migration adds `rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals()` + `_full()` SQL functions mirroring `rebuild_official_donation_totals` (FIX-237's sibling). FEC pipeline calls the incremental RPC at end-of-run after the existing PAC cross-cycle pass. One-shot `_full()` backfill runs against local + prod. Filter is `from_type='financial_entity'` + `relationship_type='donation'` (covers both `to_type='official'` FIX-181 path AND `to_type='financial_entity'` FIX-236 path; excludes `ie_support`/`ie_oppose`/`contract`/`grant`/`lobbying`). <!--id:FIX-269-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Officials profile page misses super-PAC IEs — `relationship_type='donation'` filter drops Schedule E inflows/opposition** — `/officials/[trump-id]` showed 1 donation ($500) when live `financial_relationships` for Trump's official row has 250 inflow rows totaling $322M — 99 ie_support rows ($137M, MAGA Inc / Future Forward etc.) and 150 ie_oppose rows ($185M). The page query at [apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx](../apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx) filtered to `relationship_type='donation'` exclusively, hiding all FIX-240 Schedule E rows. Parallel of FIX-269 but on the inflow side: the donor-side fix only had to rebuild a denormalized column; the officials page is fully live so the fix is purely a query/display change. PCC roll-up not required — IE rows already target the official directly via FIX-246 weball matching (`to_type='official'`, `to_id=officialId`). Fix: expand inflow query to `.in('relationship_type', ['donation', 'ie_support', 'ie_oppose'])` with `.order('amount_cents', desc).limit(2000)`, split client-side by `relationship_type`, render Top Donors / Outside support / Outside opposition as **separate sections** (uncapped Schedule E spending is politically and legally distinct from FECA-capped donations; "money supporting" and "money opposing" are opposite signals and should never be aggregated into a single "total outside spending" number). Anchor totals from prod: Bernie Moreno (elected) $14.6M donations + $67M ie_support + $86M ie_oppose; Kamala Harris (candidate) $268M donations + $1.73B ie_support + $259M ie_oppose; Schumer (no IE) unchanged at $5M donations only. <!--id:FIX-270-->
- [x] 🟢 L — **Chord multi-mode + granularity + compare + Full Senate edge bug** — Chord was locked to one hardcoded "industry → party" view inferred from props. Now exposes 8 user-selectable data modes via Settings → Data picker: industry-party (global default), industry-official, sector-group, sector-group-pair, sector-vote (donor sectors ↔ yea/nay/abstain), subject-party (bill topics × party chamber by yes-vote count), donor-type-party (Individual/PAC/Super-PAC/Corp/Union × party), state-party (donor home state × party). Added "Group by" dropdown for donor-side modes — aggregate (sectors), top-pacs (top-N PAC arcs colored by industry), by-bracket (Mega/Major/Mid/Small size brackets). Multi-official compare mode: when 2+ officials are focused, each becomes a recipient arc with shared donors visible as ribbons fanning to multiple recipients. Top N (1–50) and Min Flow ($0 → $10M, 12 log-stops) replaced with sliders that filter client-side after a single fetch — dragging never refetches (eliminated 429s on slider drag). Per-mode color palettes: vote outcomes green/red/grey, donor types distinct hues, brackets match force-graph BRACKET_TIERS. Seven new SQL RPCs in `20260509000003_chord_new_modes.sql`: `chord_sector_vote_for_officials`, `chord_subject_party_flows`, `chord_donor_type_party_flows`, `chord_donor_state_party_flows`, `chord_industry_flows_for_official`, `chord_top_pacs_for_official`, `chord_donor_brackets_for_official`. Four new presets: Sector vs Vote Outcome, Topics by Party, Donor Type by Party, Out-of-State Money. **Bug fixes folded in:** (1) entity-mode chord branch was reading the dropped `financial_entities.industry_category` column and the renamed `official_id`, returning empty for any "Industries → Official" view — replaced with `chord_industry_flows_for_official` RPC. (2) `/api/graph/group` `financial_entities` lookup was unbatched while sibling queries batched at 100 — Full Senate hit ~900 distinct donors, blew past PostgREST URI cap, returned silently empty so force graph showed only the group node and chord greyed out. Batched at 100 to match. (3) Chord registry `isApplicable` rule was too strict (required loaded edges); chord can render globally without any data, so simplified to `() => APPLICABLE` and let the per-mode picker handle gating. <!--id:FIX-221-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`OfficialCard.tsx` queries non-existent `official_id` column on `financial_relationships`** — Post-2026-04-22 cutover, donations are polymorphic on `financial_relationships`: a donation to an official lands as `(to_type='official', to_id=<official.id>)`. The `official_id` top-level column was dropped. `OfficialCard.tsx` still queries `.eq("official_id", id)` for the donor-count + total-donations totals — PostgREST silently returns `0` rows for the non-existent column, so the official card shows `0 donors` / `$0` regardless of actual data. Same class of bug FIX-223 fixed for the homepage Wave 3 (`official_homepage_stats_mv` filtered on `to_type='official' AND to_id=...` instead of the dropped column). Surfaced during the FIX-301 audit at `docs/audits/request-path-aggregations.md`; noted there as a correctness finding outside the perf scope of that audit. Two-path fix: (a) easy — change `.eq("official_id", id)` to `.eq("to_type","official").eq("to_id",id).eq("relationship_type","donation")` matching the FIX-223 MV's filter; (b) better — read `official_homepage_stats_mv` so the card uses the same precomputed numbers the homepage already trusts. Pick (b) if the MV row shape covers everything the card displays; (a) otherwise. <!--id:FIX-308-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **`proposals.procedural_contamination` regression — 1865 court/municipal rows leaking into `proposals`** — Confirmed-real data regression surfaced by [[FIX-300]] 5/19 audit (up from 827 in the 4/19 baseline, more than doubled in 5 weeks). The audit check at [packages/data/src/pipelines/integrity-audit/checks/proposals.ts:9-10](../packages/data/src/pipelines/integrity-audit/checks/proposals.ts#L9-L10) is correct (`title ~* '^on ' OR title ~* ' v\\. '`); the regression is upstream in the proposal ingest pipelines. Sample (from [docs/audits/2026-05-19.md](audits/2026-05-19.md)) shows two distinct leak shapes: (a) federal civil-case names — `Kaur v. Blanche`, `Vanicek v. Blanche`, `Castro-Lopez v. Blanche`, `Crew v. Ratliff`, `Diaz-Jimenez v. Blanche`, `Pesticide Action & Agroecology Network v. United States Environmental Protection Agency` (all `type='other'`) — likely from courtlistener or regulations-gov; (b) SF/Seattle municipal proposals with court-case names embedded in the title (`Ordinance authorizing settlement … Linda Kay Roberts v. City and County of San Francisco`, `Appointment of Todd V. Biesold as member, Seattle Freight Advisory Board`, `A RESOLUTION in support of abortion rights … Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization`) — likely from legistar. Action: (1) classify the 1865 rows by source via `metadata->>'source'` or jurisdiction; (2) for each contaminated source, tighten its title-shape filter (drop `^On ` and ` v. ` matches that aren't legislative subjects); (3) decide whether the existing 1865 should be retroactively deleted, marked archived, or left as-is and excluded from the check via a flag column. See [docs/audits/2026-05-19-triage.md](audits/2026-05-19-triage.md) for triage notes. Cross-ref [[FIX-300]] (triage parent), [[FIX-318]] (audit-check tightening for the other 4 findings). <!--id:FIX-319-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Officials ingest completeness — sitting POTUS/VPOTUS missing + House 438/441 gap** — Surfaced by [[FIX-318]] when tightening the `officials.president_count` and `officials.vp_count` audit checks. Wikidata? hardcoded canonical row?) that creates `officials` rows with `role_title = 'President of the United States'` / `'Vice President of the United States'` and `tier='elected'` — the FIX-318 audit check already matches that exact shape, so the check will flip from error→info automatically when the data lands; (2) investigate the 6 delegates' jurisdiction mapping — they should point at DC/PR/GU/VI/AS/MP territorial jurisdictions, not `"United States"` (the openstates or congress.gov pipeline is at fault); (3) decide whether the 3 House vacancies are real (current 119th Congress vacancy state) or whether the expected=441 should flex. See [docs/audits/2026-05-19-triage.md](audits/2026-05-19-triage.md) and the investigation scripts at [packages/data/src/scripts/audit-triage-investigate.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/audit-triage-investigate.ts) / [audit-triage-investigate-2.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/audit-triage-investigate-2.ts). - [x] 🟠 S — **Two more callers of dropped `financial_relationships.official_id` column** — Surfaced while shipping [[FIX-308]]. Same bug class: post-2026-04-22 cutover removed the `official_id` top-level column; PostgREST silently returns 0 rows when the filter column doesn't exist. Two remaining callers: (1) [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/sunburst/route.ts:322-326](../apps/civitics/app/api/graph/sunburst/route.ts#L322-L326) — `.from("financial_relationships").select("donor_name, amount_cents, metadata").eq("official_id", entityId).not("donor_name","ilike","%PAC/Committee%").order(...)` — the sunburst chart's donor breakdown for an official entity returns 0 rows, so the graph's donor wedge is empty. (2) [apps/civitics/app/api/officials/[id]/summary/route.ts:93-96](../apps/civitics/app/api/officials/[id]/summary/route.ts#L93-L96) — `.from("financial_relationships").select("amount_cents").eq("official_id", id)` feeds the `/api/officials/[id]/summary` aggregate sum; downstream consumers see $0. - [x] 🟠 M — **Officials ingest dedup: same person produces distinct UUIDs across mixed-case vs ALL-CAPS rows** — Surfaced by [[FIX-322]] prod audit sample on 2026-05-21. `Adam Scheinman` appears 3× and `Deborah Lipstadt` 2× in the `officials.suspicious_elected_tier` sample — same person, different UUIDs, distinguishing field is just casing. Real-world impact: search results double-count, connection-graph nodes split, financial-relationship aggregations under-count if joined on the wrong UUID. Investigate the officials ingest upsert key (`source_ids->>'X'` per pipeline source — see `packages/data/src/pipelines/` for federal/state/appointed sources) — the upsert is likely keying on `full_name` raw or a source-specific ID that varies by case. Fix: normalize casing before upsert key derivation, OR add a canonical-name column with a UNIQUE constraint. Need to surface every duplicate cluster first (SELECT lower(full_name), COUNT(*) FROM officials GROUP BY 1 HAVING COUNT(*) > 1) to scope before patching. Don't auto-merge — common-surname false positives are a real risk per [[FIX-273]] lessons. Queue-investigate-then-merge pattern. <!--id:FIX-325-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **One line fix for warren_votes_count** — warren_votes_count missing index: add .eq("from_type", "official") to the query in sections.ts. One-line JS change, expected drop ~6s → sub-100ms. Largest remaining cron-time pole. <!--id:FIX-343-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Sunburst donor wedge: donor_name is a dropped column on financial_relationships** — Surfaced during [[FIX-344]] commit 1. The 2026-04-22 cutover dropped `financial_relationships.donor_name` along with `donor_type`, `industry`, and the top-level `official_id`. The donation-industries branch in [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/sunburst/route.ts:320-328](../apps/civitics/app/api/graph/sunburst/route.ts#L320-L328) still calls `.select("donor_name, amount_cents, metadata").not("donor_name", "ilike", "%PAC/Committee%")` — PostgREST returns 42703 column does not exist, `withDbTimeout` swallows the error, route returns 0 children. Confirmed by direct PostgREST probe against local Docker: `{"code":"42703","message":"column financial_relationships.donor_name does not exist"}`. FIX-344 commit 1 (sunburst filter rewrite from `.eq(official_id)` → polymorphic `to_type/to_id`) was pushed as 0ea30f2e then reverted as f29be960 because the filter swap alone leaves the route still broken on `donor_name`. Pattern: same as the audit doc recommendation for [officials/[id]/page.tsx:285](../apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx#L285) (see [docs/audits/app_query_audit_pre_cutover.md:115](../docs/audits/app_query_audit_pre_cutover.md#L115)). Surgical scope: only the donation_industries branch on the individual-mode path; do not touch the group-mode RPC paths or the vote_categories branch. <!--id:FIX-374-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **fec_bulk PGRST116 on DC jurisdiction seed — narrow existence-check by parent_id** — seedJurisdictions() in [packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts](../packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts) uses .maybeSingle() on (fips_code, type) to detect existing state-equivalent rows. For STATE_DATA entries with type='district' (DC + the 5 [[FIX-376]] territories), this collides with TIGER-derived sub-district rows that inherit the parent's fips_code. Prod has 'District of Columbia Delegate District (at Large)' (DC-98) at fips_code='11', type='district', parent_id=DC's canonical id — second match → PGRST116. Surfaced by [[FIX-382]] Data Health visibility into fec_bulk failures. Root cause: existence check ignores hierarchy depth — top-level state-equivalents and TIGER sub-districts both match. Fix: add .eq('parent_id', federalId) to narrow the check to direct children of the federal jurisdiction. The DC-98 row is legitimate TIGER data and stays. No migration needed — pure code fix. Other callers of seedJurisdictions() (10 nightly pipelines) all hit the same swallowed-error path: stateIds.get('DC') silently returns undefined when the catch fires, so DC-related data has been silently degraded across the board on prod since DC-98 landed 2026-05-09. <!--id:FIX-383-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Territory delegate name-mismatch — Plaskett + 49 FEC candidates land on federalId** — Parallel root cause to [[FIX-383]] surfaced by the post-FIX-383 audit. Congress.gov returns `state = "Virgin Islands"` but [STATE_DATA](../packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts) has `name = "U.S. Virgin Islands"`, so the name-keyed lookup misses and the caller falls back to `federalId`. Result: Stacey Plaskett + 49 territory FEC candidates currently sit on the federal jurisdiction row instead of their territory rows. Distinct from [[FIX-383]] (which was the PGRST116 swallow on DC seed) — this is a pure name-mismatch lookup miss on the territory path. Two viable fixes: (a) one-off backfill UPDATE in the spirit of the DC backfill from [[FIX-383]] cleanup, or (b) small code change in [packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts) adding a `stateAbbr`-keyed second lookup so "VI" matches regardless of full-name string. Preferred shape is (b) + a one-shot backfill for the existing mis-attributed rows so future runs are self-healing. Affects 5 territories ([[FIX-376]] scope: VI, PR, GU, AS, MP) wherever Congress.gov / FEC string differs from STATE_DATA.name. <!--id:FIX-385-->

- [x] 🟡 S — **Backfill DC jurisdictions after FIX-383 (Norton + 34 FEC candidates)** — Restores DC jurisdiction_id on 35 officials misattributed during the [[FIX-383]] silent-degradation window (2026-05-09 → 2026-05-25). - [x] 🟠 M — **Search and summary routes filter/select bare comment_period_end on proposals (metadata-only post-cutover)** — Pattern-1 silent-zero (JSONB-path-vs-top-level-column). `apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts:377,382,383,389` selects, range-filters (.gte/.lte) and orders proposals by bare column `comment_period_end`; `apps/civitics/app/api/search/entity/route.ts:94` and `apps/civitics/app/api/proposals/[id]/summary/route.ts:76` select it too (the summary route also selects bare `agency_id`). Post-cutover both are metadata-only on proposals — migration `20260421000002_stage1_03_proposals.sql` defines no such columns and the old top-level column was dropped at the `20260422000000` promotion; migration `20260520000000` documents this exact class (bare filter silently no-op-d by PostgREST). Impact: public proposal search date-range filter and ordering silently do nothing, the comment-deadline field is always null, and the summary route never generates AI summaries because its isOpen check and agency lookup both read null. Fix: use `metadata->>comment_period_end` (expression index exists, FIX-359) and `metadata->>agency_id`. Precedent [[FIX-303]], [[FIX-359]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28, adversarially verified vs migrations. <!--id:FIX-425-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Rule-tagger selects nonexistent votes.proposal_id (real column is bill_proposal_id)** — Pattern-1 silent-zero. `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts:383` runs `.from(votes).select(official_id, proposal_id, vote)` but `proposal_id` is not a column on votes — the real FK is `bill_proposal_id` (used correctly at line 795 of the same file). So `v.proposal_id` is undefined and the `votesByOfficial` / `yesVotesByProposal` maps (lines 403-414) are keyed on undefined; the bipartisan / cross-party vote-pattern tags are computed from broken indexes and write wrong or zero tags to entity_tags (surfaced on official profiles). Fix: project `bill_proposal_id` and update the map keys. Precedent [[FIX-303]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-426-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Rule-tagger loads officials/financial_relationships/proposals unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (`supabase/config.toml` max_rows=1000). The rule-based tagger `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` loads whole tables with no .range()/.limit() then consumes them as complete Maps: `:365` officials, `:388` financial_relationships donation→official (~1M rows on prod), `:778` proposals (~73k). Past 1000 rows the loads silently truncate, so donor-pattern tags (pac_heavy/grassroots/large_donor_funded), bipartisan tags and pre_vote_timing tags derive from a tiny prefix — at ~1M financial_relationships it sees ~0.1% of donations. Fix: paginate via .range() loops (or aggregate server-side) before building the lookup maps. Precedent [[FIX-422]], [[FIX-219]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28, verified vs config.toml + migrations. <!--id:FIX-427-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Graph connections route loads entity_connections unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (max_rows=1000). `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/connections/route.ts` runs unbounded .select() on entity_connections (a derived table that scales to ~1.9M edges) at lines 239,240 (neighbor connection counts), 264,265 and 339,340 (auto-expand + top-10 edge hydration). Each result is consumed as complete — counts feed the auto-expand decision, rows feed connMap for graph rendering. For any high-degree node the result silently caps at 1000, so neighbor counts undercount (wrong collapsed/expanded state) and edges past 1000 vanish from the rendered graph with HTTP 200. Fix: add .limit(1001) overflow detection or .range() pagination per entity. Precedent [[FIX-422]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-428-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **notify-followers cron loads user_follows unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap, latent)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (max_rows=1000), latent. `apps/civitics/app/api/cron/notify-followers/route.ts:51` and `:115` load user_follows filtered only by entity_type with no pagination, then dedupe into Sets treated as the complete follower set for notification fan-out (admin client bypasses RLS, so the PostgREST 1000-row cap applies). Once any entity_type accumulates >1000 follow rows, followers past the first 1000 silently get no notifications. Dormant today (follow volume < 1k) but a correctness landmine as the platform scales. Fix: .range() pagination loop. Precedent [[FIX-422]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-429-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **ai-tagger active-officials count load unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap, cost-gate estimate)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (max_rows=1000), internal. `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-tagger.ts:616` loads active officials via `.select(id).eq(is_active,true)` with no limit (the sibling proposals load at line 613 uses .limit(2000)), then computes the untagged-officials count from `(data ?? []).filter(...).length` at line 623. If active officials exceed 1000 the count is wrong, skewing the AI-tagger cost-gate estimate (the non-onlyNew branch at line 628 already uses head:true count correctly). Internal estimate only, no user-facing data. Fix: use { count: exact, head: true } for cardinality, or paginate if the id list is needed. Precedent [[FIX-422]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-430-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **withDbTimeout callers ignore error/timeout — silent empty/zero (connections graph, homepage donor stat)** — Pattern-3 silent-zero (withDbTimeout returns data:null on timeout but callers ignore error). `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/connections/route.ts:224` throws only on donationsRes.error, then lines 226-228 merge votesRes.data and oversightRes.data blind — on a votes/oversight timeout the connection graph silently drops those edge types and still returns HTTP 200 (partial graph presented as complete); the chunked financial_entities loads at :391 have the same per-result unchecked-error issue. `apps/civitics/app/page.tsx:354` renders the homepage donor-records hero stat as 0 on timeout via `liveDonorRes.count ?? 0`. Fix: check error before consuming and surface a stale/empty state instead of a silent zero. Precedent [[FIX-327]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-431-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Institution page reads pipeline_state via non-admin client — RLS silent-zero** — Pattern-5 silent-zero (RLS). `apps/civitics/app/institutions/[id]/page.tsx:485` reads pipeline_state with the non-admin createServerClient from line 236. pipeline_state has RLS enabled and zero SELECT policies (migration `0012_entity_tags.sql:107`, commented internal-pipeline-metadata service-role-only), so the anon/authenticated read returns null with no error — plumLastChange (plum-book last-updated date on the institution page) is always blank regardless of DB state. The FIX-398 precedent table external_source_refs was since fixed (migration `20260526000002_xsr_public_read_rls.sql` added a public read policy); this is a new instance of the same class. Fix: read pipeline_state via createAdminClient for this one field. Precedent [[FIX-398]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-432-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Rule-tagger tagProposals() loads proposals unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (config.toml max_rows=1000). `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` tagProposals() ran `.from(proposals).select(...)` with no .range()/.limit() (~73k proposals) then iterated the result, so urgency/new/agency-sector/national-scope rule tags were only ever written for the first 1,000 proposals (1.4%). Distinct from [[FIX-427]], whose :778 line-ref is tagPreVoteConnections' proposal-title lookup, not tagProposals. Fixed alongside FIX-426/427: paginated via fetchAllPaged + authoritative DELETE-then-reinsert (stale time-sensitive urgent/closing_soon/new tags no longer accumulate). Verified local: 73,027 proposals now processed, 4,619 tags. <!--id:FIX-436-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Rule-tagger tagPreVoteConnections + tagFinancialEntities still load unbounded after FIX-426/427** — Remaining Pattern-2 silent-zeros in `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` not resolved by the FIX-426/427 tagOfficials rewrite. (1) tagPreVoteConnections loads financial_relationships (:746), votes (:759) and proposals (:778) unbounded — the :778/pre_vote_timing portion is explicitly named by [[FIX-427]] but needs a different fix: the donation×vote within-90-days cross-join is ~65M qualifying pairs (collapsing to ~371k distinct financial_entities) and the current code does one upsert round-trip PER pair, so naive .range() pagination would attempt ~65M upserts. Needs an SQL rollup that aggregates to distinct entities + batched upsert. (2) tagFinancialEntities loads financial_relationships (:626, from_type=financial_entity) and financial_entities (:636) unbounded → donation-size + industry rule tags computed from the first 1,000 rows only. Fix: server-side aggregation / .range() pagination as appropriate. Precedent [[FIX-427]], [[FIX-436]]. <!--id:FIX-437-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **compute_alignment_score RPC references nonexistent votes.proposal_id (real column bill_proposal_id)** — Same bug class as [[FIX-426]], different site. `supabase/migrations/20260425102000_compute_alignment_score.sql:27` joins `votes v ON v.proposal_id = cc.proposal_id`, but votes has no proposal_id column — the real FK is bill_proposal_id (confirmed vs live schema: votes columns are id, bill_proposal_id, official_id, vote, voted_at, …). The function is SECURITY DEFINER plpgsql, so the embedded query is parsed at execution → every call to the FIX-042 USER-node alignment tooltip errors at runtime with 'column v.proposal_id does not exist' (alignment_ratio/matched_votes never compute). Fix: new migration with CREATE OR REPLACE projecting bill_proposal_id (cc.proposal_id stays — civic_comments DOES have proposal_id). Found during FIX-426 schema verification. <!--id:FIX-438-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **votes.proposal_id→bill_proposal_id rename completeness — 6 read sites** — FIX-426 fixed only tagOfficials + rollup RPCs; the post-cutover rename was never applied repo-wide. This sweeps the remaining read paths — graph/matrix + graph/connections routes, OfficialCard/OfficialGraph two-step embeds, notify-followers cron, and officials/[id] page (two broken proposals!bill_proposal_id embeds the original ticket missed) — and absorbs [[FIX-438]] (compute_alignment_score join). Also fixed two sibling silent-schema breaks surfaced in graph/matrix: officials.state and officials.chamber columns do not exist post-cutover (both live in metadata) and 500'd the route before the votes fetch was even read; and graph/connections read vote_question off metadata when it is a first-class column (procedural filter was failing open). votes embeds via proposals do NOT resolve (no direct FK) — two-step manual fetch is the prescribed shape. <!--id:FIX-440-->

### DB PERFORMANCE / COMPUTE

- [x] 🟠 M — **Supabase IOWait diagnosis — read-only audit pass** — Read-only diagnostic against prod (Pro Small, 1 GB RAM, 256 MB shared_buffers) covering top IO consumers (pg_stat_statements), burst contention (pg_stat_activity sampling), working set vs shared_buffers, unused/oversized indexes, table bloat ([[FIX-346]] re-measure), cron schedule overlap, and rebuild/snapshot shape. Output: [docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md](audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md) with 12 findings ranked by expected IO impact (top 3: incrementalise rebuild_entity_connections, verify get_connection_type_counts MV is on the read path, add proposals(metadata->>agency_id) expression index). FIX-346 closable per Section F — autovacuum has caught up. Follow-up implementation FIXes will be specced from the audit in a separate Cowork session. Script preserved at packages/data/src/scripts/iowait-diagnosis.ts (pnpm data:iowait-diagnosis) for re-runs. <!--id:FIX-352-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`get_connection_type_counts()` RPC reads `connection_type_counts` table only** — Re-issue `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` for the RPC so its body is the pure 16-row read from `public.connection_type_counts` (originally landed in [[FIX-338]]), and tighten function-level `statement_timeout` from FIX-298's 120s safety net down to 5s. Audit 2026-05-24 §B #2 showed 1,028 calls × 5,992 ms × 38.9M blks — exactly the live-aggregate cost the FIX-338 materialization was supposed to remove, indicating a missed deploy or pg_stat_statements history from before FIX-338 landed. Idempotent on environments already on the materialized read path. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260524100000_fix_a_connection_type_counts_rpc_uses_mv.sql`. Expected drop on prod: ~30% of total request-path IO. <!--id:FIX-358-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`proposals` metadata expression indexes (`agency_id` re-affirmed, `comment_period_end` new)** — Add expression indexes on every `proposals (metadata->>'<key>')` that appears in a runtime WHERE filter. Two distinct shapes confirmed by codebase grep + EXPLAIN ANALYZE: (a) `agency_id` equality — already had `idx_proposals_metadata_agency_id` from [[FIX-303]], re-issued `IF NOT EXISTS` to guarantee prod presence; (b) `comment_period_end` range (`.gt` / `.lt` / `.order`) — new index `proposals_metadata_comment_period_end_expr`. Local EXPLAIN before/after on the `comment_period_end > now` shape: `Parallel Seq Scan` (11,531 buffers, 22.5 ms) → `Index Scan` (46 buffers, 0.24 ms), 250× buffer reduction. Eliminates the bulk of the 26,068 seq_scans + 1.64B tup_read of `proposals` flagged in audit 2026-05-24 §B #3 + #8 + §D hot tables. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260524100001_fix_b_proposals_metadata_expr_indexes.sql`. <!--id:FIX-359-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`entity_connections (from_id, connection_type)` compound index** — Investigation outcome: **Branch A — index missing**. `pg_indexes` on `entity_connections` shows `entity_connections_from (from_type, from_id)` leading with `from_type`, but the audit's query #9 shape (`WHERE from_id = X AND connection_type = Y LIMIT/OFFSET`) omits `from_type`, leaving the planner to do an Index Scan on `(from_type, from_id)` and per-row filter by `connection_type`. Local EXPLAIN before/after on a busy `from_id`: Index Scan on `entity_connections_from` (15,998 buffers, 117.7 ms, 2,998 rows discarded by Filter) → Index Scan on the new `entity_connections_from_id_connection_type` (3 buffers, 0.06 ms). 5,300× buffer reduction. Addresses audit 2026-05-24 §B #9 — the worst cache-hit ratio (3.9%) in the top 25 queries. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260524100002_fix_c_entity_connections_from_id_connection_type_idx.sql`. <!--id:FIX-360-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Drop 7 unused-and-large indexes (~1.05 GB working-set shrink)** — **IOWait Round 2 / FIX-A** (audit `docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md` §E). Drops seven indexes verified `idx_scan = 0` in the audit window and re-verified at ship time via `docs/audits/scratch/2026-05-24-round2-preflight.ts` against prod. Ships in `supabase/migrations/20260524230000_fix_a_drop_unused_indexes.sql` — plain `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS` with `SET lock_timeout = '5s'`. Drops: `entity_connections_strength` (468 MB), `financial_relationships_metadata_gin` (246 MB), `entity_connections_amount` (123 MB), `financial_entities_display_trgm_individual` (117 MB), `financial_relationships_occurred_at` (72 MB), `shadow_proposals_search_vector` (12 MB), `external_source_refs_metadata_gin` (11 MB). The point is structural cache-pressure relief: 256 MB shared_buffers ÷ 12.5 GB top-3-table working set ≈ 2%; freeing ~1 GB of index pages leaves more room for the indexes actually backing hot queries. <!--id:FIX-364-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`donor_fingerprint_pattern` (166 MB) kept — in active use by EDGAR matcher (FIX-B redirect)** — **IOWait Round 2 / FIX-B — closed-as-redirected.** The audit (`docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md` §E) flagged `financial_entities_donor_fingerprint_pattern` (text_pattern_ops, 166 MB) as a candidate drop pending grep. Pre-ship grep found a live `.like("donor_fingerprint", "${probe}|%")` call at `packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/matcher.ts:88` — the [[FIX-253]] EDGAR exec-to-FEC donor matcher, which uses this exact index for prefix-range scans on canonical name. Index kept; no migration shipped. Closed as `Verified: closes-as-redirected` to [[FIX-367]] for "is the pattern index hot enough to justify its size, or can the LIKE callsites be rewritten to use the unique index's equality shape?" investigation. <!--id:FIX-365-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Drop 3 true-duplicate indexes (officials, agencies, graph_snapshots)** — **IOWait Round 2 / FIX-C** (audit `docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md` §E). Three pairs re-verified byte-identical at ship time via `docs/audits/scratch/2026-05-24-round2-preflight.ts` against prod. Drops the legacy unprefixed names — `officials_full_name_trgm` (6.5 MB) and `agencies_name_trgm` (120 kB) — keeping the `idx_<table>_<col>_<type>` form (the `0008_search_indexes.sql` codebase convention). Also drops `idx_graph_snapshots_code` (8 kB, non-unique) since the UNIQUE constraint `graph_snapshots_code_key` already serves equality lookups and enforces the dedup invariant. Hygiene cleanup (~6.8 MB total); main value is removing the footgun where future renames would need to track two indexes. Ships in `supabase/migrations/20260524230001_fix_c_drop_duplicate_indexes.sql`. <!--id:FIX-366-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Investigate `donor_fingerprint_pattern` hot-ness — can EDGAR matcher's LIKE calls be rewritten to use the equality index?** — Follow-up to [[FIX-365]] (IOWait Round 2 / FIX-B closed-as-redirected). The 166 MB `text_pattern_ops` index exists solely to back `.like("donor_fingerprint", "<canonical>|%")` in the [[FIX-253]] EDGAR exec-to-FEC matcher (`packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/matcher.ts:88`). EDGAR runs weekly; per-run query count is bounded by the def14a parser output. Open questions: (1) measure actual `pg_stat_user_indexes.idx_scan` for `financial_entities_donor_fingerprint_pattern` over a weekly cycle — if it's only firing on EDGAR runs, the 166 MB amortized over a weekly hot-path workload may not be worth keeping; (2) can the LIKE prefix scan be rewritten as an equality-on-fingerprint shape if the matcher pre-computes the canonical-name fingerprint prefix and uses the existing UNIQUE `donor_fingerprint_unique` index instead; (3) does the existing canonical-name → fingerprint normalization in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts` produce enough ZIP-suffix variants per donor to make the prefix scan necessary, or are there typically only 1-3 distinct ZIPs per donor (which a small `IN ()` over equality lookups would handle equally well)? Decision criteria: if EDGAR-run `idx_scan` is < 1000/week, drop the pattern index and rewrite the matcher to use either the unique index or a small range of equality probes. <!--id:FIX-367-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Close FIX-367 — donor_fingerprint_pattern index analysis** — Investigation work for [[FIX-367]] — does the 166 MB `text_pattern_ops` index on `financial_entities.donor_fingerprint` justify its working-set cost vs rewriting the EDGAR matcher to use an equality-shape index? Outcome will be either (a) keep the index, close FIX-367 as `closes-as-recognized` because the prefix scan is structurally necessary, or (b) allocate a new FIX-D for the matcher rewrite and close FIX-367 as `closes-as-redirected`. Either way, the rewrite itself is out of scope for this PR. <!--id:FIX-371-->

### OFFICIALS

- [x] 🟠 M — **Merge officials casing dupes — execute the merge plan from FIX-325 investigation** — Reads the cluster shape from docs/audits/<date>-officials-casing-dupes.md (FIX-325 investigation script) and executes the merge. Pick the winner row per cluster preferring (in order): most external_source_refs bindings, longest first_name+last_name string (richer detail preserved), lowest UUID for determinism. Rewrite FK references — use the FK rewrite block from supabase/migrations/20260525051720_executive_seed_and_delegate_jurisdictions.sql Block C as the canonical pattern, generalized across the cluster rather than a single (elected,candidate) pair. **Out of scope, do NOT auto-merge:** (a) clusters whose tier-array contains both 'elected' AND 'candidate' for different real people — [[FIX-248]] promotion handles that pair specifically; (b) FEC candidate cycle-row clusters (e.g. Chris Capparell 7× across 7 cycles — same person, different fec_candidate_id per cycle is the FEC norm, not a bug) — these need a per-cycle dedup strategy that preserves the historical cycle binding rather than a blanket merge. Local 5/25 baseline: 1,829 dupe clusters / 4,108 rows. Top sources by row count: fec_candidate_id (1,955 rows), openstates_id (267), plum_id (202), congress_nomination_id (112), fec_id (88), congress_gov (78). Pattern to mirror: [[FIX-271]] cross-source resolution backfill. <!--id:FIX-375-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **us-states.ts STATE_DATA: seed 5 territorial jurisdictions (AS/GU/MP/PR/VI) so congress writer routes territorial delegates correctly** — **Shipped in same PR as [[FIX-321]] data fix — filed for completeness/audit.** Adds the 5 U.S. territory entries (American Samoa, Guam, Northern Mariana Islands, Puerto Rico, U.S. Virgin Islands) to packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts STATE_DATA with type='district' (mirrors DC's existing convention — jurisdiction_type enum has no 'territory' value). This is the upstream writer-side counterpart to the migration in [[FIX-321]] Block B: before this fix, congress/officials.ts at line 131 (stateIds.get(member.state) ?? federalId) fell through to the 'United States' umbrella jurisdiction for territorial delegates because seedJurisdictions only ever seeded the 50 states + DC. The migration creates the 5 jurisdiction rows + re-routes the 5 misrouted delegate rows AS A ONE-SHOT; this writer fix ensures the next congress sync (and every subsequent one) keeps them mapped correctly. No code change in congress/officials.ts itself was needed — the existing stateIds.get(member.state) lookup uses full state names which Congress.gov returns verbatim for territorial reps (e.g. 'Puerto Rico', 'Guam'). <!--id:FIX-376-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Eleanor Holmes Norton (DC) misrouted to 'United States' jurisdiction on prod** — Adjacent finding surfaced during [[FIX-321]] prod verification (5/25). Local already had Norton at the DC district jurisdiction (fips=11, type='district'); prod had her at the federal 'United States' umbrella (fips=00, type='country'). Root cause: on prod, the congress.gov officials ingest wrote her to federalId at a time before OpenStates' DC pass had landed (DC, like the 5 territories prior to FIX-376, wasn't in STATE_DATA initially as a state — only added as type='district' later). Once the writer's stateIds.get('District of Columbia') started resolving, new congress runs would have written her correctly, but the existing prod row was never UPDATEd. Shipped as a one-shot migration alongside the FIX-321 PR: supabase/migrations/20260525053643_norton_dc_delegate_jurisdiction.sql. Idempotent guard: only fires when she's currently at the federal umbrella. <!--id:FIX-377-->

NON-AI ingestion work. **OpenStates abstracts** — add `include=abstracts` to the existing `/bills` call (`openstates/index.ts:118-132`), parse `abstracts[].abstract` into `summary_plain`; **no extra API calls** (rides current requests), ~3,356 actionable, partial state coverage. **Congress CRS summaries** — new `/bill/{congress}/{type}/{number}/summaries` call per bill (batchable, daily nightly, `CONGRESS_API_KEY`), real multi-paragraph prose, ~2,795 actionable; highest yield. Regulations.gov detail-endpoint `summary` is sparse (real text is PDF behind `full_text_url`) — lower priority. Writes `proposals.summary_plain` → requires a prod backfill re-run (data-state change, run local+prod separately). Confirm each API actually supplies the text before building. Recommended order: OpenStates (free) → Congress CRS → reassess regulations. <!--id:FIX-435-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Capture OpenStates abstracts into `summary_plain` (FIX-435 Phase 1)** — Non-AI. Ride `include=abstracts` on the existing `/bills` call (`openstates/index.ts`), parse `abstracts[].abstract` (longest non-empty) into `proposals.summary_plain` via the writer, COALESCE-don't-clobber-non-null on both the insert and update paths (`openstates/writer.ts`). Local verified: `full_summary` 0→114 from a partial ~5-state run. Caveat: pipeline re-runs only backfill the recently-updated window (`sort=updated_desc`, `MAX_BILL_PAGES=3`), not the full corpus. Phase 1 of [[FIX-435]]; Phase 2 (Congress CRS summaries) stays open under FIX-435. <!--id:FIX-445-->

(c) Add the agencies source key(s) to source_priority() SQL function AND resolveSource() registry ([[FIX-399]]) so the badge resolves a proper label + category. Agencies are only 100 rows so the seed is trivial once the source is identified. Cross-ref [[FIX-397]] (materialization), [[FIX-399]] (SourceBadge that needs this data), [[FIX-402]] (governing_bodies — the sibling gap on the other table). <!--id:FIX-410-->

Stage 3 (table collapse, agencies removal, 31 callsite refactor, slug backfill, /institutions index) are separate follow-up FIXes. <!--id:FIX-418-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **/institutions/[id] governing-body substance + middleware UUID guards for /districts /officials /proposals** — Two pieces under one ID, building on [[FIX-418]] (institutions view) and [[FIX-424]] shared cards. (1) Fills in governing-body-typed sections on /institutions/[id]: jurisdiction+parent breadcrumbs, party-balance bar (legislature/municipal types), members roster via shared OfficialRosterCard (50 visible + show-all details), committees/sub-bodies tree, recent votes with party-line + unanimous indicators (new get_institution_recent_votes RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public,extensions, 3s statement_timeout), recent proposals, recent meetings via shared MeetingCard. Agencies-typed retains existing rich content from FIX-418 (untouched). (2) Extends [[FIX-433]] middleware UUID-format guard to /districts/[id], /officials/[id], /proposals/[id] (single combined regex). /institutions/[id] middleware guard DEFERRED by design: its page resolves non-UUID slugs to UUIDs via a governing_bodies.slug DB lookup, which the edge can't replicate, so a format guard would 404 legitimate slug URLs — the page keeps not-found ownership. SCHEMA-REALITY CORRECTIONS vs the spec (verified live, not 0001): votes has NO governing_body_id/proposal_id — it carries bill_proposal_id + official_id + roll_call_id + vote_question (a real column, not metadata->>); votes scope to an institution via officials.governing_body_id. officials.party is a lowercase enum (democrat/republican/independent/...), not 'Democratic'/'Republican'. Live vote values are yes/no/not_voting/present only. bill_number lives in bill_details(proposal_id), not metadata->>legis_num (unpopulated). institution_extensions is empty (0 rows) so the committees tree self-omits until backfill. notFound-under-Suspense inventory confirmed: 4 affected routes (jurisdictions already fixed by FIX-433; districts/officials/proposals fixed here; institutions deferred); donors/[id] + initiatives/[id] are NOT affected (no loading.tsx, so notFound already returns a real 404). VERIFICATION: pnpm build clean; migration applies local; RPC returns correct party-line/unanimous in ~500ms on US Senate; all page queries run clean against live DB; middleware guards return 404 for malformed and let slugs through. Local browser render of /institutions/[id] is blocked by a PRE-EXISTING env issue (Cannot find module ./vendor-chunks/@smithy+core — pnpm + Next 14 next start/dev vendor-chunk bug, also flagged by Invalid next.config serverExternalPackages warning); confirmed via stash test that ORIGINAL code 500s identically, so unrelated to this change. Prod render to be confirmed via post-deploy curl. RPC migration to prod (schema change) pending explicit user approval. <!--id:FIX-439-->

- [x] 🟢 S — **Add R2 cache layer for FEC bulk files** — Follow-up to FIX-181. The indiv pipeline currently downloads `indiv{yy}.zip` (~2 GB) from `fec.gov/files/bulk-downloads` on every run. R2 plumbing exists in [packages/db/src/storage.ts](packages/db/src/storage.ts) but is unused by the FEC pipeline. Add a HEAD-based freshness check: on each run, HEAD `civitics-cache/fec/indiv{yy}.zip` in R2 + HEAD the FEC URL; if R2 is fresh (Last-Modified ≥ FEC's), download from R2 instead. After successful FEC download, upload to R2 in the background. Saves ~10 minutes per repeat run + insulates against FEC bulk-download outages. Requires `@aws-sdk/lib-storage` for multipart upload. Same pattern can be retrofitted to pas2/cm/weball. Defer until cadence justifies it (pipeline runs more than once a quarter). <!--id:FIX-192-->
