- [x] 🔴 L — **Surface individual donors in search + ingest super-PAC indiv contributions (Form 3X Schedule A)** — Anchor symptom: Elon Musk's ~$277M to America PAC (2024) is in the public FEC bulk feed but does not appear in Civitics search. Two independent root causes ganged up: (a) [api/search/route.ts:484-489](../apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts#L484-L489) unconditionally excluded `entity_type='individual'` whenever no entity_type filter was set, making all ~540K individual donors invisible by default — fixed by only excluding individuals in pure browse mode (no query + no industry/amount filter); search also now tries the reversed `LAST, FIRST` form of any 2-token query so "Elon Musk" matches FEC's stored `MUSK, ELON`. (b) [fec-bulk/indiv.ts:200-203](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts#L200-L203) filtered every indiv line through `cmteToCandId` (ccl P/A only), silently dropping individual contributions to super PACs, party committees, and other non-candidate-authorized PACs — fixed by adding a parallel `nonCandCmtes` set (CMTE_TP O/X/Y/Z/N/Q/V/W in cm{yy} but NOT in ccl P/A) and routing those lines into a new (donor × committee × cycle) aggregation. New writer `upsertIndividualToCommitteeDonationsBatch` writes them as `financial_relationships` rows with `to_type='financial_entity'`, `source='fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee'`. No SQL migration — schema and unique-index arbiter already cover the new `to_type` combo. `rebuild_entity_connections()` derives the donor → super_PAC graph edge automatically on the next nightly rebuild. Joint-fundraising (J), leadership (D), and B designations stay excluded — their money is re-itemized via transfers downstream and would double-count. **Prod data backfill requires `pnpm data:fec-bulk` against prod to land the new super-PAC indiv rows** (the next Sunday cron picks it up automatically). <!--id:FIX-236-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Normalize donor `canonical_name` to natural-order + add trgm GIN index** — Donor `canonical_name` is currently set from the FEC `LAST FIRST` fingerprint (writer.ts:217), so a natural-order search like "Elon Musk" can't use an index-served prefix match — FIX-236 worked around it with a per-query OR fallback that escapes commas. Cleaner long-term: reorder to `FIRST LAST` form at write time, add `CREATE INDEX … USING GIN(canonical_name gin_trgm_ops)`, and have search query `canonical_name` directly. Requires a one-off backfill of the ~540K existing individual rows (run as a `data:` script — SELECT id, fingerprint → UPDATE canonical_name = reorder(fingerprint)). Display_name stays as the raw FEC source-cased value. <!--id:FIX-238-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Donor `donor_fingerprint` fragmentation by middle initial + ZIP changes** — `donorFingerprint = upper(NAME) + "|" + ZIP5`. "MUSK, ELON @ 94027" and "MUSK, ELON R @ 94027" become two `financial_entities` rows; "MUSK, ELON @ 94027" and "MUSK, ELON @ 78704" (donor filed from a second residence) also split. Empirically observed during FIX-181 ingest — top-donor totals are split across 2–4 fingerprint variants for many high-frequency donors. Decide on a dedup approach (strip middle initial / suffix? cluster by name + city + employer? add a `dedup_cluster_id` derived nightly?). Investigate before backfilling — wrong heuristic can collapse genuinely-distinct people (common surname + same first name in same ZIP). <!--id:FIX-239-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Ingest FEC `oth{yy}.zip` + `oppexp{yy}.zip` for super-PAC → candidate independent expenditures** — After FIX-236, donor → super_PAC edges land cleanly but super_PAC → candidate edges are missing for IE-only super PACs (the majority of them). America PAC's $277M was spent supporting Trump via independent expenditures, none of which appear in pas2 (24K/24Z transactions). `oth{yy}.zip` carries IE transactions (Schedule E) and `oppexp{yy}.zip` carries detailed operating expenditures. Stream them line-by-line like indiv, aggregate by (committee × candidate × cycle), upsert as `financial_relationships` with `relationship_type='donation'`, `metadata.source='fec_bulk_ie'`. Closes the graph trail from any megadonor through their super PAC to the candidate(s) the PAC supports. <!--id:FIX-240-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Verify next Sunday FEC cron lands green on GHA after FIX-192/193 + heap bump** — FIX-192 (R2 cache), FIX-193 (FEC_INDIV_CYCLES + Last-Modified watermark), and the [.github/workflows/nightly.yml](../.github/workflows/nightly.yml) heap/timeout bump (NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192, timeout-minutes=120) all ship as one bundle and are locally verified. The first real prod confirmation comes on the next Sunday cron (2026-05-17, 02:00 UTC). What to check on the run: (a) job completes without timeout, (b) no OOM, (c) `pipelines.fec_bulk.status='complete'` in `pipeline_state.cron_last_run`, (d) `pipeline_state.fec_indiv_watermark` rows for 2026 (and 2024 only if `FEC_INDIV_CYCLES` was overridden), (e) R2 cache keys `fec/2026/{weball26,cm26,pas226,ccl26,indiv26}.zip` exist in `civitics-cache`. If R2 secrets aren't yet set as GitHub repo secrets the code degrades to direct FEC fetch — fine, just no cache benefit. On success, append a `Verified: prod` trailer to a follow-up commit referencing this FIX.  (redirected to FIX-292 — verification ran, finding became its own FIX) <!--id:FIX-242-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **`normalizeName()` strips apostrophes into whitespace, splitting `O'BRIEN` into two tokens** — Surfaced in [FIX_239_INVESTIGATION.md](FIX_239_INVESTIGATION.md) §2.5, §8. [packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts) `normalizeName()` replaces every non-alphanumeric (including apostrophes) with whitespace, then collapses runs of whitespace. Surnames like `O'BRIEN`, `D'ANGELO`, `M'CARTHY` tokenize as `O BRIEN` / `D ANGELO` / `M CARTHY`, so `first_token` becomes `O` / `D` / `M` instead of the real surname. ~5,000 individual donor rows affected with spurious leading tokens: `O` (2,965 rows), `DE` (933), `D` (403), `ST` (265), `MC` (181), `M` (176). Fix: replace apostrophes (and periods) with **empty string** (NOT whitespace) before the non-alphanumeric replacement step, so `O'BRIEN` → `OBRIEN` and `M.D.` → `MD`. Logically adjacent to FIX-239 Layer 1 — bundle in the same PR. <!--id:FIX-244-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Ingest FEC candidate master (cn{yy}.zip) + `tier` column on officials** — FIX-240 closed the donor → super-PAC → candidate trail in `financial_relationships`, but the candidate vertex doesn't exist as an `officials` row for non-incumbents, so `rebuild_entity_connections()` can't materialize the graph edge for losing candidates and the presidential trail (America PAC → Trump is the anchor case). Fix: (a) migration `20260510000007_official_tier.sql` adds `tier TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'elected' CHECK (tier IN ('elected','candidate'))` + an index — existing rows default to `'elected'`; (b) new `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts` streams `cn{yy}.zip` from `https://www.fec.gov/files/bulk-downloads/{YYYY}/cn{YY}.zip` and inserts new `tier='candidate'` officials for every H/S/P candidate not already present (dedup via `source_ids->>'fec_candidate_id'`, application-layer pre-fetch map); (c) `seedGoverningBodies` extended to seed an `Office of the President of the United States` row (`type='executive'`) so presidential candidates have a governing_body FK; (d) candidate stage runs **before** weball matching so newly-inserted rows benefit weball's fec_candidate_id resolution path on first ingest; (e) `apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx` hides Promises / Career History / Recent Votes / Votes tab when `tier='candidate'`, adds a small amber "Candidate" badge next to the party chip; (f) search route needs no change (no tier filter exists; chamber filter intentionally narrows to incumbents). Verified local: 9,802 candidate rows for cycle 2024 (Trump P80001571, Bernie Moreno, J. Vance, Frank LaRose etc. all surface). `'former'` tier and auto-promote-on-win are future work. <!--id:FIX-246-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Candidate name parsing: "J Vance" instead of "JD Vance"** — FIX-246's `parseFecName` took only the first whitespace-separated token after the comma in FEC's `LAST, FIRST [MIDDLE]` format. JD Vance's FEC entry is `VANCE, J D` → first_name was `J`. Fixed by extending [parseFecName](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/util.ts) to collect any leading run of single-letter tokens (tolerating trailing `.` or `,`) and joining them with periods: `J D` → `J.D.`. Two+ leading initials produce `X.Y.` form; a single leading initial stays as `X`; multi-char first names are unchanged. [streamCandidates](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts) was extended with a name-update path: it pre-fetches existing `tier='candidate'` rows and UPDATEs any whose stored `full_name`/`first_name`/`last_name` differ from what the current parser yields. Elected rows are excluded — their names are owned by congress/openstates. Verified local: J.D. Vance, A.C. Toulme, C.L. Smith, J.R. Majewski etc. (26 candidates renamed in cycle 2024). Verified prod: 28 cn24 + 16 cn26 candidates renamed; J.D. Vance row carries `first_name='J.D.'`, `full_name='J.D. Vance'` on prod. <!--id:FIX-247-->
- [x] 🔴 L — **LittleSis.org relationship-graph ingest (CC-BY-SA 4.0)** — New pipeline at [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/) (index.ts, util.ts, matcher.ts, expand.ts, writer.ts, README.md). LittleSis tracks ~600K relationships between powerful people + organizations (inverse of LinkedIn). Two bulk dumps fetched from `https://littlesis.org/database/public_data/{entities,relationships}.json.gz`; SHA256 fingerprinted into `pipeline_state.littlesis_state` so subsequent runs skip via `skipSync('dumps_unchanged')` until LittleSis publishes new dumps. Scope: 2-hop expansion from existing Civitics entities (officials + financial_entities) — NOT the full dump. Schema (3 migrations dated `20260511000000`-`02`): (a) connection_type enum extended with `member_of`, `owns`, `parent_of`, `affiliated_with` (4 new values); (b) `external_relationships` table polymorphic edge buffer (UNIQUE(source, source_id) for idempotency); `external_relationships_review_queue` for ambiguous matches; (c) `rebuild_entity_connections()` extended with block 10 that aggregates `external_relationships` per (from,to,connection_type) with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so authoritative blocks (career_history appointment, FR lobbying_spend) stay authoritative on overlap. Function gets `#variable_conflict use_column` directive to dodge the OUT-param / table-column ambiguity. Category mapping: cat 1 Position→`appointment`, cat 3 Membership→`member_of`, cat 6 Transaction→`business_partner`, cat 7 Lobbying→`lobbying`, cat 10 Ownership→`owns`, cat 11 Hierarchy→`parent_of`, cat 12 Generic→`affiliated_with`. Cat 5 Donation is DROPPED at ingest — FEC bulk is authoritative for campaign donations. Matching is deterministic-only: reuses `canonicalizeEntityName` (mandatory cross-pipeline normalizer from fec-bulk/writer.ts), builds 3 in-memory indexes (officials by last_name, individuals by alphabetical-token-sort key, orgs by canonical_name) and narrows by state hint parsed from LittleSis `types`/`aliases`. HIGH = exact match, MEDIUM = single survivor after narrowing, anything else → review queue (FIX-252 drains). Person sort-key lets "Elon Musk" (LittleSis FIRST-LAST) match "MUSK ELON R" (FEC LAST-FIRST). Hop-1 entities inserted as `financial_entities` with `[LS:<id>]` suffix on individual canonical_name to avoid common-name collisions; bound via `external_source_refs(source='littlesis', external_id=<ls_id>)`. UI attribution is FIX-252 (no current UI surfaces this). License attribution in code header (`index.ts`), migration comment, and README. Wired into weekly Sunday block after FEC + IRS-990 so the matcher sees fresh entities. Verified local + prod, anchor cases McConnell / Musk / Thiel / Koch / Bessent. <!--id:FIX-251-->
- [x] 🔴 L — **IRS Form 990 bulk ingest — officers + grants-out for politically-active nonprofits** — Form 990 doesn't disclose donors (Schedule B is redacted in the public e-file distribution), but it does carry officers/directors/key employees (Part VII Section A), grants OUT (Schedule I), and financial summary. Goal: illuminate dark-money **network structure** by linking 501(c)(4) / (c)(6) / 527 / politically-active (c)(3) orgs to existing officials via shared board members, and to other ingested orgs via grants-out. New pipeline at [packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/](../packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/) (index.ts, writer.ts, util.ts, parse.ts, seed.ts), mirroring fec-bulk's structure. Primary source: IRS-hosted bulk ZIPs at `https://apps.irs.gov/pub/epostcard/990/xml/{YEAR}/index_{YEAR}.csv` (AWS S3 bucket frozen Dec 2021 — ignored). Schema: migration `20260510000008_irs990.sql` adds `'nonprofit'` to financial_entities.entity_type, creates `irs990_filings` (UNIQUE on IRS DLN object_id), `irs990_officers` (UNIQUE filing_id+name_canonical+role_title), `irs990_grants_out` (UNIQUE filing_id+recipient_name_canonical+amount_cents). `rebuild_entity_connections()` block 6 (holds_position) UNIONs in officer rows whose matched_entity_id resolved against the `officials` table. Officer matching is conservative — officials-only, exact-canonical-name; donor-side matching deferred to Phase 2. Seed list: ~35 politically-active orgs in [packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/seed.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/seed.ts) (Americans for Prosperity, One Nation, NRA, LCV, AFT, U.S. Chamber, Heritage, CAP, etc.). Default tax years: `[CURRENT_YEAR-3, CURRENT_YEAR-2, CURRENT_YEAR-1]` (override via `IRS990_TAX_YEARS`). Wired into weekly Sunday block after FEC bulk so resolveGrantRecipient sees freshly-upserted PAC entities. 990 XML schema versions handled via tag-name-only traversal (`fast-xml-parser` with `removeNSPrefix`). Out of scope (Phase 2 FIXes): full-bulk crawl, UI surfacing, donor-side officer matching, 990-PF private foundations, officer co-board edges. <!--id:FIX-250-->
- [x] 🔴 L — **Cross-source entity resolution foundation (Strategy D)** — Per [docs/CROSS_SOURCE_RESOLUTION_INVESTIGATION.md](CROSS_SOURCE_RESOLUTION_INVESTIGATION.md). Two migrations land the foundation for Sessions 2/3 ingest patches: (a) `20260514000000_cross_source_resolution.sql` adds `entity_cluster_id UUID` (generic-named per investigation §8 to share with FIX-239 Layer 2) + `resolve_entity_by_canonical(canonical, entity_type, state)` SQL helper that returns single UUID iff exactly one row matches; (b) `20260514000001_cross_source_backfill.sql` one-shot destructive merge mirroring FIX-239 Layer 1's set-based DELETE+INSERT pattern. Two scopes per investigation §5.4: individuals with exactly 1 FEC-fingerprinted row + ≥1 non-FEC row (winner = FEC row to preserve `donor_fingerprint` UNIQUE); non-individual canonical clusters with ≥2 mergeable rows (`fec_committee_id IS NULL` excludes FEC PACs which stay distinct) where ≥1 carries a `littlesis | irs_990 | sec_edgar | usaspending_recipient` binding (winner = most non-FEC bindings, tiebreak `total_donated_cents DESC`). Multi-FEC indiv clusters (multi-ZIP fragmentation, ~6k local) deferred to FIX-239 Layer 2; the 203 PAC+LS distinct-ID clusters per investigation §2.6 stay separate. Backfill rewrites `from_id`/`to_id` across all 9 FK-bearing tables (financial_relationships set-based aggregate to dodge `relcycle_unique` collisions; external_relationships / external_source_refs / edgar_* / irs990_* straight UPDATE), merges loser totals into winner, deletes losers, truncates `entity_connections` for the rebuild. **Local result:** 8,577 indiv + 19,544 org clusters, 38,677 losers deleted, 47s wall-time. **Prod result:** 3,393 indiv + 628 org clusters, 4,037 losers deleted, ~66s wall-time. Anchor-case win: prod ELON MUSK is now ONE row carrying FEC `donor_fingerprint` (`MUSK ELON|78734`) + LittleSis binding `LS:38805` + 23 FEC donations + **142 LittleSis edges** — the 132-edge LittleSis Musk and the FEC Musk are the same entity. THE HERITAGE FOUNDATION 3 → 1 (nonprofit + 'other' + LS collapsed). 0 FK orphans, 0 UNIQUE collisions on either env. Application code (donor profile, search, graph) reads `financial_entities.id` and picked up the merged identity automatically; `entity_cluster_id` stays NULL until a future cluster-rebuild job populates it. PITR captured 2026-05-14T22:54:52Z before prod push. <!--id:FIX-271-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **`[LS:<id>]` suffix on LittleSis individual canonical_name is either dead code or pre-dates the data load** — Investigation §9 OOS #1. [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/writer.ts:107](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/writer.ts#L107) documents an `[LS:<ls_id>]` suffix on individuals' canonical_name to dodge cross-source collisions, but 0 of 84,811 LS-bound individual rows in local DB carry the suffix — meaning every LS individual collides on canonical_name with any FEC indiv of the same name (the root cause exhaustively explored in CROSS_SOURCE_RESOLUTION_INVESTIGATION.md). Either the running pipeline matches an older revision of the writer than what's on disk, or the suffix branch is unreachable. Action: `git blame writer.ts:107` to date the suffix code, run `pnpm data:littlesis` against a clean local DB and confirm the suffix DOES land on new rows. If the suffix code is reachable, the pre-FIX-271 LS rows just need a one-shot retroactive suffix backfill. If unreachable, fix the branch. Either way, FIX-271's resolve_entity_by_canonical helper makes the suffix less critical going forward (Sessions 2/3 patches will lookup-then-bind instead of unconditionally INSERTing) but the code drift is worth resolving for clarity. <!--id:FIX-272-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **LittleSis intra-source duplicate pairs (~50 cases) — same person, two LittleSis ids** — Investigation §9 OOS #2. Paul Singer has both LS:59970 and LS:52680 in `financial_entities`; ~50 such pairs visible in local data (646 intra-source-only multi-row indiv clusters as of 2026-05-14, including non-LS sources). Either LittleSis ships duplicate entities in the bulk file or [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/matcher.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/matcher.ts)'s lookup-by-LS-id pre-pass isn't catching them. Action: query `external_source_refs WHERE source='littlesis'` for entities sharing canonical_name + entity_type='individual'; quantify, then either dedupe at ingest (preferred, since LS publishes a `merged_into` field on their entity records) or run a one-shot intra-source merge similar to FIX-271's plan. Out of FIX-271 scope because cross-source merge requires a different winner heuristic (FEC row preferred); intra-source needs LittleSis-side tie-breaker (latest updated_at, or higher edge count). <!--id:FIX-273-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **FEC indiv ingest creates `entity_type='individual'` rows for org names** — Investigation §9 OOS #3. AMERICANS FOR PROSPERITY exists as 2 individual rows (FEC donors filed the org name in their NAME field), and similar artifacts exist for ONE NATION etc. These leak into search and graph rendering as fake "individual donors". Action: [packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts) adds an org-name guard before insert — a small static blacklist of known org names + a heuristic detector (e.g. canonical_name contains "INC", "LLC", "PAC", "FOUNDATION", "ASSOCIATION", "SOCIETY", "FUND", or matches an existing IRS 990 nonprofit's canonical). Misclassified rows already in the DB → one-shot merge into the existing nonprofit/org row of the same canonical (fold into FIX-271's pattern as a follow-up backfill). FIX-271 explicitly leaves the 2 AfP indiv rows untouched per spec. <!--id:FIX-274-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **LittleSis anchorMap matches never written to `external_source_refs`** — Investigation §9 OOS #4. When [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/matcher.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/matcher.ts) `matchPerson` returns `kind='high'` or `kind='medium'`, the anchor is added to in-memory `anchorMap` and the LS hop-1 INSERT is skipped — but the anchor binding (LS_id → existing financial_entity_id) doesn't appear to be written back to `external_source_refs(source='littlesis', external_id=<ls_id>)`. `preloadKnownLittleSisIds` reads existing bindings, so the next ingest re-fetches the same LS id and re-runs matching. Probably a real bug; could explain part of the 8,577-case "single-FEC-match miss" cohort the investigation flagged in §3.4(a). Action: read [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/index.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/index.ts) anchor handling end-to-end; if the binding-write step is missing, add it inside the same transaction as the matchPerson resolution. After FIX-271's merge, this fix becomes more impactful — Sessions 2/3 ingest patches that use `resolve_entity_by_canonical` should similarly bind into `external_source_refs` immediately. <!--id:FIX-275-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Nightly orchestrator order: FEC bulk should complete before LittleSis starts** — Investigation §9 OOS #5 + §3.4(a) (the 8,577 single-FEC-match misses). [packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts) `runNightlySync` schedules LittleSis after FEC, but the LittleSis matcher's `personsBySortKey` index is built at LittleSis pipeline START — if a freshly-inserted FEC indiv row lands AFTER that index build, the matcher misses it and the LS row goes to hop-1 INSERT. With FIX-271 merging cross-source duplicates retroactively, this is less critical now but still produces transient duplicates within a single nightly run that the next backfill has to clean up. Action: confirm LittleSis pipeline depends on FEC pipeline completion in the orchestrator (already true per the file structure) AND that the in-memory matcher index is built AFTER all FEC writes are flushed. Cheap to verify; high-leverage if there's a race. <!--id:FIX-276-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **IRS 990 ingest patch: canonical-name fallback before INSERT (Session 2 of FIX-271 forward-looking patches)** — Investigation §3.3 + §5.3. [packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/writer.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/writer.ts) `upsertNonprofitEntity` previously checked only `external_source_refs(source='irs_990', external_id=EIN)` before INSERT — meaning every new EIN created a fresh `financial_entities` row even when LittleSis hop-1 or USAspending recipient already had the same org canonical under `entity_type='other'` or `'corporation'`. The AfP/Heritage/Chamber duplication pattern Session 1 (FIX-271) just collapsed would re-emerge on the next Sunday cron. Patch: after the EIN-miss path and before the bare INSERT, call `resolve_entity_by_canonical(canonical, p_entity_type=null)`. On single-match: write the EIN binding to `external_source_refs` against the matched entity_id and return; do NOT update the matched row's `entity_type`/`metadata` (preserves prior source attribution; 990 financial summary lands in `irs990_filings` sidecar via `insertFiling` regardless). On NULL (miss or ambiguity per RPC's single-match-only contract): existing INSERT path unchanged. RPC failure logged and falls through to INSERT — safer than crashing ingest. `p_entity_type=NULL` is the brief's recommendation: catches matches under any non-individual type (FEC PACs are naturally safe via `fec_committee_id` UNIQUE; investigation §7.5 covers the 203 PAC+LS distinct-ID clusters that stay separate). No migration — patch is TS-only, relies on Session 1's RPC helper. Verified local: re-run leaves the 17 existing EIN-bound nonprofits untouched (step 1 short-circuit). Prod verification deferred to 2026-05-17 Sunday cron. <!--id:FIX-277-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **EDGAR companies ingest patch: replace local canonical helper with `resolve_entity_by_canonical` + loosen entity_type filter to 'corporation' + 'other' (Session 2 of FIX-271 forward-looking patches)** — Investigation §3.4 + §5.3. [packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/companies.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/companies.ts) `findFinancialEntityByCanonical` filtered to `entity_type='corporation'` only, missing matching LittleSis hop-1 or USAspending rows under `entity_type='other'` — investigation §3.4 notes LittleSis writes corporations under either type depending on `types[]` shape (`littleSisOrgEntityType` fallback at [littlesis/util.ts:330-338](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/util.ts#L330-L338)). Patch: drop the local `.eq("entity_type","corporation").maybeSingle()` helper and replace with `resolveCorporationEntityByCanonical(db, canonical)` that calls the FIX-271 RPC twice sequentially — first with `p_entity_type='corporation'`, then `'other'` — returning the first non-null match. Sequential not parallel: each type window is evaluated independently against the RPC's single-match-only contract, so a multi-match within a type window returns NULL (ambiguity → safe fall-through to INSERT) but a single 'corporation' AND a single 'other' that exist simultaneously still resolve cleanly to the 'corporation' row. Verified single-callsite via grep before changing the helper; only [companies.ts:256](../packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/companies.ts#L256) (now using the new wrapper) consumes it. No migration — patch is TS-only. Replaces the local helper with the central RPC for consistency with Session 1's contract (one source of truth for cross-source matching). Verified local: re-run leaves the existing CIK bindings untouched (preloaded `bindings` map short-circuits before reaching the new wrapper). Prod verification deferred to 2026-05-17 Sunday cron. <!--id:FIX-278-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **IRS 990 grants-out: aggregate same-recipient-same-cycle grants within a filing before upsert (two collision sites, one root cause)** — Surfaced during FIX-277 local verification on 2026-05-14. A single 990 filing can carry multiple Schedule I grant lines to the **same canonical recipient** in the same tax year (e.g., quarterly disbursements of identical or different amounts, per-state chapter grants of identical amounts, recurring program grants). Two downstream constraints reject these batches wholesale: (a) [supabase/migrations/20260510000008_irs990.sql:127](../supabase/migrations/20260510000008_irs990.sql#L127) `irs990_grants_out` UNIQUE(filing_id, recipient_name_canonical, amount_cents) — when two grant lines share canonical recipient + amount, [packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/writer.ts:351-383](../packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/writer.ts#L351-L383) `upsertGrantsOutBatch`'s single-statement `.upsert(..., {onConflict})` fails with `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE command cannot affect row a second time`, dropping the entire batch; (b) [supabase/migrations/20260423000000_financial_relationships_donation_unique.sql:20](../supabase/migrations/20260423000000_financial_relationships_donation_unique.sql#L20) `financial_relationships_relcycle_unique(relationship_type, from_id, to_id, cycle_year)` — when two grants to the same recipient have the same cycle_year (any amount), [packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/writer.ts:407-465](../packages/data/src/pipelines/irs990/writer.ts#L407-L465) `writeGrantRelationships`'s `.insert(rows)` collides regardless of amount. Concrete impact from the 2026-05-14 local re-run: AMERICAN MEDICAL ASSOCIATION 2025 filing lost 142 grants_out rows + 25 financial_relationships rows; smaller losses elsewhere across the seed set (199 failures total). Action: in both writer paths, aggregate by (filing_id, recipient_name_canonical) for `irs990_grants_out` and by (filing_object_id, toEntityId, taxYear) for `financial_relationships` BEFORE the upsert/insert — sum amount_cents, preserve count in metadata, keep first purpose (or concat unique purposes if cheap). Mirrors the FEC PAC pipeline's per-(committee × candidate × cycle) aggregation pattern. No schema change needed — the aggregation is application-layer; the UNIQUE keys are correct for the aggregated shape. Out of scope for FIX-277 because the bug pre-dates Session 1 and the local-only runtime confirmation surfaced after the patch landed; queuing as a separate fix to keep the FIX-277 commit narrow. <!--id:FIX-279-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **LittleSis hop-1 canonical-resolve patch + `resolve_entity_by_canonical` max(uuid) bugfix (Session 3 of FIX-271 forward-looking patches)** — Investigation §3.2 + §5.3 + §9 OOS #1. Two things bundled into one commit because the second blocks the first. **(1) RPC bugfix.** Session 1's `resolve_entity_by_canonical` (migration `20260514000000`) used `SELECT count(*), max(id) INTO v_count, v_id FROM public.financial_entities …` to detect single-match. Postgres 17.6 has no `max(uuid)` aggregate, so the function threw `function max(uuid) does not exist` on every match and never returned a non-NULL UUID. Session 2's IRS 990 + EDGAR patches (FIX-277/278) gracefully degraded to INSERT-everything on RPC error so the bug was silent — but the patches haven't actually prevented any cross-source duplicates since they shipped on 2026-05-15. New migration [supabase/migrations/20260517000000_fix_resolve_entity_by_canonical_max_uuid.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260517000000_fix_resolve_entity_by_canonical_max_uuid.sql) replaces the count+max trick with a `LIMIT 2 + array_agg + array_length` pattern that's UUID-safe; signature, return type, and contract unchanged. **(2) LittleSis hop-1 patch.** [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/writer.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/writer.ts) `upsertHop1FinancialEntities` previously did a bare INSERT per chunk of 500 entities, applying a `[LS:<id>]` suffix to individual canonical_names. New helper `resolveOrInsertHop1` calls `resolve_entity_by_canonical` first — single-match → bind via `external_source_refs` instead of INSERTing a duplicate; NULL → fall through to INSERT under the base canonical. Persons use `p_entity_type='individual'` (RPC's `donor_fingerprint IS NOT NULL` filter excludes LS-only individuals from accidental cross-pollution under common names); orgs use `p_entity_type=NULL` to allow LS 'corporation'/'other' to bind to existing 'nonprofit'/'corporation'/'other' rows under the same canonical (Heritage Foundation case per investigation §5.6), mirroring Session 2's IRS 990 pattern. `[LS:<id>]` suffix code removed — match-first via RPC supersedes its collision-prevention purpose, and 0 of 84,811 production LS-bound individuals carried it pre-patch (investigation §9 OOS #1). Caller [packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/index.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/index.ts) log line gains `matched=` and `rpcErrors=` counters for observability; binding metadata stamps `confidence='canonical_match'` for RPC-resolved bindings vs `'hop1'` for new inserts. Bounded concurrency `RESOLVE_BATCH=50` replaces the prior 500-row INSERT chunk (the RPC + INSERT path is per-row and parallelizes naturally). Idempotency for previously-bound LS ids stays upstream via `preloadKnownLittleSisIds` + `if (known.has(lsId)) continue` at index.ts:154 — no LS id reaches the new helper after a successful prior binding, so the IRS 990 Stage 1 `external_source_refs` pre-check is unnecessary here. No prod backfill — forward-looking. **Local result (2026-05-17 re-run):** `inserted=119,275 matched=19,660 failed=0 rpcErrors=0` — 19,660 cross-source duplicates prevented in a single run. Anchor cases: ELON MUSK / ELON MUSK REVOCABLE TRUST / THOMAS PETERFFY each 1 row (unchanged). Multi-FEC residue stays as expected for FIX-239 Layer 2 (JOHN ARNOLD 14→16, M JUDE REYES 3→4, PAUL SINGER 6→7 — RPC returns NULL on multi-match per single-match-only contract, falls through to INSERT). 0 rows with `[LS:]` suffix post-patch. RPC smoke-test post-fix: PETERFFY single-match returns UUID, SINGER multi-match returns NULL, missing canonical returns NULL, empty returns NULL. **Verified local** — prod verification deferred to 2026-05-17 Sunday cron (also retroactively unblocks FIX-277 + FIX-278 on Pro). <!--id:FIX-280-->
- [x] 🔴 L — **SEC EDGAR ingest — executives + major shareholders (S&P 500)** — Supersedes FIX-213 (which mis-specified `data.sec.gov/submissions/{CIK}.json` as a source of officer rosters — submissions JSON only carries filing history, not officers). New pipeline at [packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/](../packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/) (index.ts, client.ts, companies.ts, def14a.ts, shareholders.ts, matcher.ts, writer.ts, util.ts, universe.ts, data/sp500.csv). V1 universe: S&P 500 (503 tickers) from a curated CSV at `data/sp500.csv` — refresh annually from the `datasets/s-and-p-500-companies` mirror. Sources: per-company submissions JSON (metadata), DEF 14A bodies (officer roster + Summary Compensation Table), daily full-index for SC 13D/13G/A (major-shareholder events). 10-K fallback parsing and 13F institutional holdings are deferred to follow-up FIXes. Schema: (a) `20260512000000_edgar_schema.sql` — `edgar_companies` UNIQUE(cik), `edgar_executive_officers` UNIQUE(company_id, canonical_name, effective_year, role_category), `edgar_major_shareholders` UNIQUE(accession, canonical_holder_name); (b) `20260512000001_donor_fingerprint_pattern_index.sql` — adds `text_pattern_ops` index on `financial_entities.donor_fingerprint` so the matcher's LIKE 'prefix|%' uses the index instead of seq-scanning 558k rows (the existing FIX-239 UNIQUE btree without text_pattern_ops can't serve prefix scans under the default en_US.UTF-8 collation). No connection_type enum changes, no rebuild_entity_connections() changes — edges land in `public.external_relationships` with `source='edgar'` and reuse existing values: `'appointment'` for exec-of (mirrors LittleSis cat 1 Position) and `'owns'` for major shareholder (mirrors LittleSis cat 10 Ownership). Block 10 of `rebuild_entity_connections()` materializes both. Matcher rule (conservative — Craig's explicit answer): exact `normalizeName(officer.full_name) == normalize(donor.display_name)` AND `canonicalizeEntityName(donor.metadata->>'employer') == company.canonical_name`. Probes BOTH name orderings (FIRST-LAST and LAST-FIRST) because FEC stores `donor_fingerprint` as `LAST FIRST|ZIP` while EDGAR officer rosters are FIRST LAST — until FIX-238 reorders donor canonical the matcher has to bridge. Weaker matches (name-only, no employer match) → `external_relationships_review_queue` with `reason='name_match_no_employer'`; `too_many_lastname_hits` mirrors LittleSis. Review queue uses `source_relationship_id = source_entity_id` (not NULL) so re-runs are idempotent under the UNIQUE(source, source_entity_id, source_relationship_id) constraint. Person-shareholder matching uses the same rule; org-shareholder matching is deferred (queue reason `'org_holder_unmatched'`). Two scripts: `pnpm data:edgar` (weekly Sunday DEF 14A reconciliation), `pnpm data:edgar:daily` (daily 13D/G poll against the SEC daily full-index). SEC fair-access: `client.ts` enforces the required `"Civitics Platform civitics.platform@gmail.com"` User-Agent and a sliding-window 10 req/sec ceiling across all stages. New TS dep: `node-html-parser`. Exports `normalizeName` from `fec-bulk/indiv.ts` so EDGAR can match on the donor-side fingerprint canonical without forking. Wired into nightly orchestrator after LittleSis. **Anchor-batch result (5 CIKs, prod):** Apple's DEF 14A SCT parses cleanly (5/5 NEOs); Luca Maestri auto-links to his Apple-employer FEC donor row; Tim Cook + Kate Adams route to the review queue (same name + different employer — likely different people, conservative threshold did its job). JPMorgan / Lockheed / Pfizer / Disney use multi-table SCT layouts the V1 single-table heuristic doesn't catch — log+skip with `unparseable=4/5`. Parser improvements (multi-table SCT, name-only-cell + title-only-cell layouts, 10-K fallback, R1000 universe, 13F) are follow-up FIXes; coverage will improve incrementally. Verified local + prod. <!--id:FIX-253-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Split nightly-sync into FEC + Enrichment phases (GHA two-job)** — Nightly cron cancelled at 2h0m on 2026-05-14 and 2026-05-17, wall-clock timeout (not OOM). LittleSis canonical binding consumes the rest of the post-IRS-990 budget. Split runNightlySync into `--phase=fec` (regulations → IRS 990) and `--phase=enrichment` (LittleSis → tag-industry → refresh_*). Two GHA jobs in nightly.yml, each timeout-minutes: 120, job 2 needs job 1. Both phases write their own `data_sync_log` row tagged metadata.phase. Implicitly closes FIX-276 (orchestrator ordering). Surfaced by FIX-242 verification. <!--id:FIX-292-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Phase 2 overwrites Phase 1's `pipeline_state.cron_last_run` blob — dashboard loses FEC/IRS-990 status fields after Sunday split** — FIX-292 split nightly into FEC + enrichment phases, each invoking `runNightlySync()` and writing `cron_last_run` via upsert at `packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts:946`. Each upsert replaces the entire `value` blob, so Phase 2's write clobbers Phase 1's pipelines map. Two read sites affected: `apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts:201` (Data Health card) + `:553` (`connections_pipeline_healthy` self-test). Canary at `data_sync_log` unaffected. Fix: read existing row first; if `value.started_at` UTC date matches current phase's `startedAt` date, merge `results.pipelines` and `results.errors`; otherwise replace. Derive merged `status` from merged errors count. Add top-level `phase` field for debuggability. <!--id:FIX-293-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Particle-prefix surnames: residue from FIX-244 (space-separated / backtick variants)** — FIX-244 shipped apostrophe + period stripping, which collapsed pure `O'BRIEN` correctly and took the leading-`O ` surface from ~2,965 rows down to 60. The 60 leftover rows are written without a real apostrophe: `O BRIEN, MICHAEL` (space-separated), `O' BRIEN, JAMES` (apostrophe + space), `` O`BRIEN, HALLEY MARIE `` (backtick instead of apostrophe). FIX-239 Layer 1 can't see those as a single token; would need either (a) backtick added to the `['.]` strip set, plus (b) recognition of the leading `O `/`D `/`DE `/`ST `/`MC ` particle pattern that joins those tokens to the next when followed by uppercase ASCII. Risk: aggressive particle-joining could fuse legitimate first-token mononyms or two-word surnames. Keep narrow: only join when the leading token is exactly `O`, `D`, `DE`, `ST`, `MC` AND the surname is at position 0 (before the comma). Same applies symmetrically to `DE LA RENTA` etc. — multi-token particle clusters. Scope is small (~60–500 rows total across the 5 particles) so worth deferring until donor-aggregation prompts care. <!--id:FIX-245-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Auto-promote `tier='candidate'` → `tier='elected'` when a candidate wins** — FIX-246 leaves both rows in place: when a candidate wins, the next `congress/officials.ts` run creates a separate `tier='elected'` row keyed on `bioguide_id`, while the `tier='candidate'` row stays for historical reference. This creates two officials rows per winning candidate. Cleaner long-term: detect overlap by (name + state + role) and unify by either promoting the candidate row's tier + adding the bioguide_id, or by retiring the candidate row to a "former_candidate" status. Bumps into the dedup ambiguity FIX-239 wrestles with (same name + state may be different people across cycles), so think carefully before automating. **Confirmed user-visible 2026-05-23**: search RPC for "warren" returned 3 Elizabeth Warren rows (Senator + MA Candidate for Senator + US Candidate for President) with identical trigram similarity; `entity_search_finds_warren` self-test was matching the candidate slot (6 vote_yes edges) instead of the Senator (606 edges). Band-aid landed in resolver (prefer subtitle without "Candidate for"); the durable fix is FIX-248. Priority bumped 🟢→🟠 because this pattern likely affects every sitting senator/rep with a 2024 or 2026 candidate filing. <!--id:FIX-248-->
- [x] 🟢 M — **UI attribution for LittleSis-sourced relationships (CC-BY-SA 4.0)** — Follow-up to FIX-251. CC-BY-SA 4.0 requires user-facing attribution for any UI surface that exposes LittleSis-derived data. Today no page renders `evidence_source='external_relationships'` or surfaces the LittleSis source directly, so the obligation is dormant — but it kicks in the first time the graph snapshot UI shows an edge whose evidence_source traces back to LittleSis. Add a "Sources & Licenses" section to `/about` (create it if missing) listing: "Some relationship data from LittleSis (<https://littlesis.org>), licensed CC-BY-SA 4.0." Plus a small "i" tooltip on connection-graph edges whose source is LittleSis-only. Same treatment will be needed for future CC-BY-SA sources (OpenCorporates etc.), and could potentially be implemented for all data sources in general, so factor the rendering helper accordingly.<!--id:FIX-252-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Backfill: merge AfP/ONE NATION/etc. misclassified indiv rows into matching org row** — Follow-up to FIX-274. FIX-274 closed the ingest-time path so new FEC indiv rows whose NAME field carries an org name are dropped, but ~2 individual rows already exist for `AMERICANS FOR PROSPERITY` (investigation §2.5), 2 for `ONE NATION`, plus heuristic-positive existing rows like `DEMOCRACY ENGINE LLC`, `SENATE CONSERVATIVES FUND`, `ACROSS THE AISLE PAC` (2-3 rows each in local DB per FIX-274's pre-fix audit query). Action: one-shot merge mirroring FIX-271's pattern — for each canonical that matches `isLikelyOrgName` AND has both an `entity_type='individual'` row and a non-individual row (`nonprofit`/`pac`/`other`/`corporation`), rewrite the indiv row's FK references (external_relationships, financial_relationships, external_source_refs, irs990_*, edgar_*, entity_connections) to point at the non-individual winner, then delete the indiv row. Tie-breaker when multiple non-individual rows exist: prefer the one with an `external_source_refs` binding (irs_990/littlesis/etc) over unattributed. Verification: post-backfill, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM financial_entities WHERE entity_type='individual' AND isLikelyOrgName(canonical_name)` should drop to 0 (modulo edge cases the heuristic missed). <!--id:FIX-312-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Backfill: merge LittleSis intra-source duplicates via merged_into field** — Follow-up to FIX-273. FIX-273 closed the within-batch race that creates new intra-source dupes, but ~3,310 multi-FE clusters of LS-bound individuals sharing canonical_name remain in local DB (4,017 total LS-bound canonical clusters with 8,797 rows). The vast majority are common-surname false positives — LittleSis legitimately tracks distinct people named "Robert Smith" etc, and auto-merging by canonical alone would collapse real people. Two precise paths: (a) extend `LittleSisEntity` parser at `packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/util.ts:36` to capture the `merged_into` field LittleSis publishes when they merge entities on their side, then for any LS entity in our DB where source data says `merged_into=<other_ls_id>` route the bind to the canonical id; (b) shared-edge heuristic — for clusters of 2 LS-bound FE rows sharing canonical, merge only when their `external_relationships` neighborhoods overlap above a threshold (e.g., ≥2 shared neighbor entities), strong signal of same real person. Path (a) is preferred — uses LittleSis's own authoritative dedup signal. Verification: count of multi-FE LS-bound canonical clusters drops to the residual count where LS legitimately publishes distinct people of the same name. <!--id:FIX-313-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **`data_sync_log` enrichment-phase row shows empty `metadata.pipelines` despite Phase 2 running real work** — Surfaced during FIX-292/FIX-293 prod validation 2026-05-19 against the 5/18 06:15 UTC cron run. The `pipeline_state.cron_last_run` blob (read by dashboard Data Health) merged correctly via FIX-293, but inspection of `data_sync_log` rows for that cron showed the row tagged `metadata.phase='enrichment'` with the top-level `metadata.pipelines` map appearing empty — even though Phase 2 ran 8.16 min of actual work (edgar_daily, openstates_bulk_people, etc. all executed). The orchestrator at `packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts:946+` writes `metadata: { ...results, peak_rss_mb }` which should spread `results.pipelines`. Investigate: (a) is the field landing under a different metadata path (`metadata.results.pipelines`?), (b) is Phase 2's `results.pipelines` actually empty at write time despite work being done, or (c) is this a column-naming drift in data_sync_log. Two read consumers — canary at `packages/data/src/scripts/canary-check.ts` and possibly other diagnostics. Bug or naming drift, this is a second-surface observability gap that wasn't caught because the dashboard reads `pipeline_state.cron_last_run` (which works correctly post-FIX-293). <!--id:FIX-307-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Backfill: org-misclassified indiv merge — relaxed gate accepting fec_committee_id attestation** — Follow-up to FIX-312. The 2026-05-25 investigation (docs/audits/2026-05-25-entity-backfill-bundle-{local,prod}.md) confirmed 16 local / 26 prod canonical clusters where an org-named row (`isLikelyOrgName()` positive) coexists with an `entity_type='individual'` row. The FIX-312 strict gate required every cluster's non-individual row to carry an `external_source_refs` binding (irs_990 / littlesis / sec_edgar / usaspending_recipient) — only ~4 of the 16 local clusters meet that; the rest are FEC PACs attested via `fec_committee_id` instead. **Relaxed gate**: ship the merge when the non-individual row has xsr binding OR has `fec_committee_id IS NOT NULL`. A PAC with a real fec_committee_id is as authoritative as an LS-bound nonprofit (both come from authoritative bulk pipelines). Action: re-run `pnpm data:investigate-entity-backfill-bundle` to confirm counts (~26 prod), then ship the FIX-312-style block as a delta migration following the [[20260514000001_cross_source_backfill]] / [[20260525065710_entity_backfill_bundle]] FK-rewrite pattern. Tiebreaker when multiple non-individual rows exist: prefer most xsr bindings, then `total_donated_cents DESC`. Verification: post-backfill `SELECT canonical_name FROM financial_entities fe WHERE entity_type='individual' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM financial_entities fe2 WHERE fe2.canonical_name = fe.canonical_name AND fe2.entity_type<>'individual')` should drop to 0 (modulo any isLikelyOrgName misses). <!--id:FIX-379-->

### DASHBOARD

- [x] 🔴 S — **Dashboard admin-content leaks via Vercel edge cache** — `/dashboard` is edge-cached for 30 min (`next.config.mjs` `cdnHot(1800, 3600)` rule). Vercel doesn't vary on cookies, so any admin-rendered SSR HTML — `KillSwitchBanner` events, the `ManualMetricsPanel` rows (separately removed by [[FIX-348]]) — gets cached and served to subsequent anonymous visitors for the cache lifetime. Symmetric leak in reverse: anon HTML cached → admin sees stripped page until expiry. Surfaced 2026-05-24 when admin content rendered for Craig in dev despite the dev cookies path having no edge cache (a stale Supabase refresh-token cookie made `getUser()` resolve to admin even when not signed in). Also surfaced two broken admin gates in scope: `window.CIVITICS_ADMIN` was read in 3 places (`PlatformCostsSection.tsx:58`, `ModerationSection.tsx:9`, `ManualMetricsPanel.tsx:18`) but set nowhere, so the per-metric Verify/Update buttons and the entire ModerationSection had been silently dead. Fix: SSR HTML carries no admin-only content; `KillSwitchBanner` becomes self-fetching client-side (drops the `events` prop, GETs `/api/admin/kill-switches/recent` on mount); new `/api/admin/me` cookie-based gate route + shared `useIsAdmin()` hook in `apps/civitics/src/lib/use-is-admin.ts` replaces the three inline broken hooks; `dashboard/page.tsx` drops `cookies()` + `createServerClient` + `getRecentKillSwitchEvents` + `getManualMetrics`. Each admin API route 404s for non-admins so admin content never reaches the wire. <!--id:FIX-347-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Remove `ManualMetricsPanel` + `/api/admin/usage-update`** — Added by [[FIX-296]] to surface stale manual `platform_usage` rows with a freshness badge, but it's not actionable (no alerting, just visual decay) and was the immediate symptom that surfaced the edge-cache leak fixed in [[FIX-347]]. The buried per-metric Update button inside `PlatformCostsSection`'s Supabase service-card expanded view (restored by [[FIX-347]] via the shared `useIsAdmin` hook) remains the manual-update fallback. The egress metric the panel exposed will autopopulate once Supabase Prometheus integration lands (separate forthcoming PR), eliminating the last meaningful manual entry. Delete `apps/civitics/app/dashboard/ManualMetricsPanel.tsx` and `apps/civitics/app/api/admin/usage-update/route.ts` (sole caller); the other admin update path (`/api/platform/usage` POST with `X-Admin-Key`) stays. `platform_usage.stale_after_days = 14` on the egress row stays for `SourceDisplay`'s isStale logic. <!--id:FIX-348-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Data Health card cadence + alias + window fixes** — The dashboard Data Health card on /dashboard?tab=operations diverges from /admin/pipeline-health in three load-bearing ways. Surfacing case: TIGER Districts last ran 16 days ago. /admin/pipeline-health shows it correctly (within the 30-day MV window). Data Health reads `pending / never` because (1) [getPipelines()](../apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts) pulled `data_sync_log ORDER BY completed_at DESC LIMIT 100` so high-frequency writers (ai_summaries, tag_ai, tag_rules, regulations, congress_officials) monopolised the window and pushed annual-cadence pipelines like `tiger_districts` out; (2) [pipelineFreshness()](../apps/civitics/app/dashboard/DashboardClient.tsx) hard-coded a 48h ok / 168h warning threshold, so TIGER (annual by design) returned `error` past 7 days even when healthy; (3) [DataHealthSection](../apps/civitics/app/dashboard/DashboardClient.tsx)'s multi-alias rollup (Congress = officials + votes + committees) flatMap'd alias history into one merged list and lost per-alias identity, so the row badge went green if *any* alias completed in 48h — masking a stale or failing sub-pipeline. Three bundled sub-fixes: (a) add `cadence` field to `PipelineDef` (hourly/daily/weekly/monthly/quarterly/annual/on_demand/continuous) and refactor freshness to cadence-aware thresholds; right-column timestamp prefixes `Current · ` on healthy slow-cadence rows so 16d-old TIGER reads as healthy at-a-glance. (b) replace global LIMIT 100 with (pipeline ASC, completed_at DESC) + 14-month window + 3000-row safety limit + JS-side per-pipeline bucketing so every registered pipeline with a run in window appears reliably. (c) worst-status alias rollup — Congress.gov row reads red when any of {congress_officials, congress_votes, congress_committees, congress} is stale or failing; expanded panel adds per-alias sub-pipeline breakdown with ← propagating marker on the alias that drove the verdict. Cadence ground truth validated against [.github/workflows/nightly.yml](../.github/workflows/nightly.yml), [rebuild-entity-connections.yml](../.github/workflows/rebuild-entity-connections.yml), and a 90-day prod audit of inter-run gaps via [packages/data/src/scripts/pipeline-cadence-audit.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/pipeline-cadence-audit.ts). Adjustments from initial proposal: `courtlistener` daily→weekly (CLAUDE.md confirms weekly via Sunday block); `legistar` weekly→on_demand (CLAUDE.md: "Manual only"); `usaspending` monthly→weekly + dropped legacy `usaspending` alias (FIX-249 deprecation shipped); `ai_summaries` + `tag_ai` continuous→on_demand (work moved to enrichment_queue subagent drain, no longer writes data_sync_log). <!--id:FIX-382-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Data Health card cleanup — committees split + orphan triage + script commit** — Cleanup pass after [[FIX-382]] surfaced ~10 orphan pipeline rows. Splits congress_committees out of Congress.gov (was aliased under daily, runs weekly — read false-red). Registers irs990, littlesis, edgar, edgar_daily, agency_leadership, agency_enrichment, opm_fte, plum_book as real pipelines with audit-verified cadences (weekly except agency_enrichment monthly and edgar_daily daily). Adds HIDDEN_PIPELINES set in getPipelines() to hide canary_check and nightly_cron wrapper-bookkeeping rows server-side. Hyphen-typo writer orphans (agencies-hierarchy, tag-rules) left as-is — writers were already normalized to snake_case in 845aa31a (2026-05-01), historical rows will age out of the 14-month window. Commits the FIX-382 verification helper as a permanent diagnostic at pnpm diag:pipeline-history. <!--id:FIX-384-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Site Activity card always empty - getActivity selects wrong column** — getActivity() in apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts selects 'path' from page_views; column is 'page'. PostgREST 42703 silently returns data:null, section() only catches throws, so top_pages collapses to [] and the card shows 'No activity data' even with a healthy view count in the header. Shipped wrong on day one (c0ef2a84, 2026-03-22); FIX-082's helper extraction inherited it. Fix: select 'page', alias to 'path' at the return boundary to keep ActivitySectionData/ActivityRow shapes stable. Also throw on PostgREST .error so future silent-zero regressions surface via partial:true instead of looking like genuine empty data. <!--id:FIX-394-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Site Activity: widen window to 7d and drop / and /dashboard filter** — After FIX-394 fixed the column-name bug, the card still renders empty because the 24h window combined with the .not('page','in','(/, /dashboard)') filter excludes all current human traffic. Switch the activity helper to a 7d window and stop filtering — at current volume the card is more useful with home/dashboard hits included than empty. Rename payload field page_views_24h → page_views and add lookback_days so the description text can stay accurate if we ever change the window again. <!--id:FIX-395-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Dashboard hydration mismatch — formatRelativeTime and toLocale* without suppressHydrationWarning** — React minified error #425 firing in prod on /dashboard. formatRelativeTime() (packages/ui utils) and direct toLocaleString/toLocaleTimeString/toLocaleDateString calls compute against Date.now() and the runtime's timezone during SSR, then again during client hydration with a slightly different now() and different TZ. Existing pattern at DashboardClient.tsx:1595 uses suppressHydrationWarning + a mounted gate; apply suppressHydrationWarning to the ~8 other sites that emit relative/locale-formatted text (lines ~583, 733, 735, 883, 919, 976, 1164, 1201, 1206). Tight per-element scope so unrelated mismatches still surface. <!--id:FIX-396-->

### INFRASTRUCTURE & PERFORMANCE

- [x] 🟡 M — **Pipeline runtime + memory observability from `data_sync_log`** — `data_sync_log` captures `started_at`/`completed_at`/`status` per pipeline run but nothing surfaces the trends. As pipelines scale (FEC indiv ~80M rows/week, USASpending bulk multi-GB), watching p95 duration per pipeline is the only way to catch any creeping toward the 60-min GHA job cap or Pro pooler exhaustion before they fail outright. Add (a) a `pipeline_runtime_stats_mv` materialized view with `pipeline`, `p50/p95/max duration_ms`, `success_rate`, `last_30d_runs` — refreshed nightly; (b) a `/admin/pipeline-health` page rendering the table with red/amber/green tiering against the 60-min ceiling; (c) optionally fold the same data into the weekly audit report (FIX-226). See [PIPELINE_AUDIT.md](PIPELINE_AUDIT.md) Open Question #4. <!--id:FIX-233-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Alert when Vercel cron canary fires but GHA nightly never lands** — Two cron paths write to `data_sync_log` daily: Vercel `/api/cron/nightly-sync` writes a `triggered` row at 2:00 UTC (canary, no pipeline work), and GitHub Actions writes a `nightly_cron` row when `runNightlySync()` completes. If GHA dies (auth expired, runner outage, OOM), only the canary row exists — silent failure. Add a check that (a) runs once daily at ~5 UTC (3h after the canary), (b) selects canary rows from the last 7 days with no matching `nightly_cron` row landing within 4h, (c) on mismatch, surfaces an admin-dashboard banner and/or emails `ADMIN_EMAIL` via Resend. Implementation: Vercel cron `0 5 * * *` hitting `/api/cron/sync-canary-check` (simpler than a Postgres trigger, rate-limit-safer than a GHA job). See [PIPELINE_AUDIT.md](PIPELINE_AUDIT.md) Open Question #6. <!--id:FIX-234-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Fix tsc error: `cache` property on RequestInit in `packages/db/src/supabase-usage.ts`** — Line 119 passes `cache: "no-store"` to `fetch()` (correct behavior — opts out of Next.js fetch caching for the live Supabase Management API call), but `packages/db/tsconfig.json` correctly excludes the DOM lib (server-only package), so the Node-native `RequestInit` type in scope doesn't declare `cache`. tsc errors with TS2353. Pre-existing — surfaced during FIX-234 typecheck run. Inline cast at the call site to `RequestInit & { cache?: RequestCache }` — localized, no global type pollution. Do NOT add `"dom"` lib to the package's tsconfig; this is a server-only package and DOM globals would pollute downstream consumers. <!--id:FIX-235-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Edge-cache `/api/search` + `/api/search/entity` responses** — FIX-236 made search the front door for donor discovery, but neither [apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts](../apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts) nor [apps/civitics/app/api/search/entity/route.ts](../apps/civitics/app/api/search/entity/route.ts) emits a `Cache-Control` header today — every distinct query hits Supabase fresh, gated only by the 30 req/min/IP in-memory rate limit in [apps/civitics/middleware.ts:114](../apps/civitics/middleware.ts#L114). During a viral surge thousands of distinct IPs searching the same trending name each generate a fresh DB hit. Add `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300` to the 200 responses of both routes (matches the pattern already used by [api/graph/snapshot/route.ts:1200](../apps/civitics/app/api/graph/snapshot/route.ts#L1200) and the treemap routes). Both routes use `createAdminClient()` and emit identical responses for anon vs authed users, so `public` is safe. Keep `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` — it controls Next.js build-time/ISR behavior, the response header applies at the Vercel edge, they coexist cleanly. Error responses (400/404/500) deliberately stay uncached. <!--id:FIX-243-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Raise nightly `NODE_OPTIONS` to `--max-old-space-size=12288`** — The 2026-05-12 cron OOM'd mid-stream on the 2024 FEC indiv stage at the 8 GB V8 ceiling. `packages/data/CLAUDE.md` already documents 12 GB for presidential cycles (2024/2028); the workflow was lagging the guidance. ubuntu-latest runners ship 16 GB so 12 GB still leaves real headroom. Single-line bump at [.github/workflows/nightly.yml:40](../.github/workflows/nightly.yml#L40) plus a comment refresh. See `docs/audits/cron_run_2026-05-12.md` §11. <!--id:FIX-254-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Stranded `data_sync_log status='running'` rows on OOM/SIGTERM** — When Node aborts uncatchably (V8 OOM = SIGABRT, SIGKILL, hard crash) the in-flight pipeline never reaches `completeSync()`/`failSync()` and its row strands in `status='running'` forever, polluting `data:status` and the Data Freshness dashboard (one such row was left behind by the 2026-05-12 cron). Two-layer fix: (a) process-level signal handler in [packages/data/src/pipelines/sync-log.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/sync-log.ts) tracks in-flight log ids and best-effort `failSync(id, 'node-aborted-{signal}')` on SIGTERM / SIGINT / SIGHUP / uncaughtException / unhandledRejection (2s budget, then exit); (b) new `reap_stale_sync_log(stale_minutes)` RPC ([migration 20260512100000](../supabase/migrations/20260512100000_reap_stale_sync_log.sql)) flips rows older than threshold to `failed` with `error_message='reaped_orphan'`, idempotent, called from `runNightlySync` entry + standalone `pnpm data:reap-sync-log`. V8 OOM (SIGABRT) and SIGKILL are uncatchable from JS — the reaper RPC is the only recovery for those cases (documented inline). See `docs/audits/cron_run_2026-05-12.md` §10/§11. <!--id:FIX-255-->
- [x] 🔴 L — **Chunked `rebuild_entity_connections()` by connection_type** — Post-FIX-236, financial_relationships holds 4.1M donations + 1.3M contracts + 88K grants + IE rows. The monolithic TRUNCATE+rebuild exceeded the 20-min outer session statement_timeout twice in the 2026-05-13 audit Run #2, leaving entity_connections frozen at 1,914,433 rows (FIX-236 donations not in the graph). Split into 10 per-connection-type functions ([migration 20260513000000](../supabase/migrations/20260513000000_chunked_rebuild_entity_connections.sql)) — `_donations` (60min timeout, ~4M edges, includes ie_support fold-in + recipient_count update), `_votes`, `_cosponsors`, `_appointments`, `_oversight`, `_holds`, `_gifts`, `_contracts` (30min, ~1.3M), `_lobbying`, `_external` (runs last, ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING for LittleSis overlap). Orchestrator at [packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts:816](../packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts#L816) calls each chunk as a separate top-level statement with statement_timeout reset to 45min between calls — each chunk gets a fresh wall-clock budget. Per-chunk DELETE replaces the global TRUNCATE, so live reads on /officials/[id] and /donors/[id] are no longer blocked by an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock for the entire rebuild window. Partial failure isolation: if one chunk times out, subsequent chunks continue. Umbrella `rebuild_entity_connections()` preserved for PostgREST local-dev callers. **Prod result:** 4,577,596 edges in 71.97min (donation 60min/3.85M, votes 8min/481K, contracts 40s/61K, external 3.4min/183K). Verified local + prod. See `docs/audits/cron_run_2026-05-12.md` §11. <!--id:FIX-263-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`financial_entities.name` column drift since 2026-04-22 cutover** — The shadow→public cutover ([20260422000000](../supabase/migrations/20260422000000_promote_shadow_to_public.sql)) replaced `financial_entities.name` with `canonical_name` + `display_name`. Two callsites silently broke and surfaced in the 2026-05-13 audit Run #2: (a) `tag_ai` pipeline failed every nightly run with `column financial_entities.name does not exist`, masking real AI-tagger issues; (b) [packages/data/src/scripts/cron-run-anchor-verify.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/cron-run-anchor-verify.ts) broke 5+ donor anchor queries. Both updated to `display_name` (preserves source casing — needed for AI prompt input and uppercase ILIKE patterns from FEC indiv source data). Verified local + prod. See `docs/audits/cron_run_2026-05-12.md` §10/§11. <!--id:FIX-265-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Move `/api/platform/usage` off the request path into a 10-min snapshot** — Operations dashboard (`/dashboard?tab=operations`) is slow because the GET handler recomputes the full vendor-API + DB-sum aggregation on every request: Anthropic spend SUM, Supabase Management API HTTP, Supabase SQL self-metrics RPC, `getPlatformUsage()` join, per-service plan overrides. Plain table (not MV — SQL views can't express external HTTP). New migration [20260517020000_platform_usage_snapshot_and_kill_switches.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260517020000_platform_usage_snapshot_and_kill_switches.sql) adds `platform_usage_snapshot (id, fetched_at, any_critical, any_warning, total_overage_cost, payload JSONB, error TEXT)` with `fetched_at DESC` index and a `prune_platform_usage_snapshot()` function (30d retention, called from `runNightlySync`). New `/api/cron/platform-snapshot` route (every 10 min via `.github/workflows/platform-snapshot.yml` — Vercel Hobby blocks sub-daily cron, GHA curls the route with `CRON_SECRET` instead) runs the aggregation via the shared `computePlatformUsagePayload`/`writePlatformUsageSnapshot` helpers in [packages/db/src/platform-snapshot.ts](../packages/db/src/platform-snapshot.ts) and INSERTs a snapshot row. GET refactored to read the latest row via `withDbTimeout`, falling back to a live recompute if the snapshot is missing or older than 20 minutes (= two missed cron ticks). Response shape unchanged — `PlatformCostsSection.tsx` is untouched. Edge cache `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300` matches the FIX-243 pattern. PR 1 of 3 (platform monitoring overhaul); PR 2 adds Cloudflare + Vercel Usage API metrics, PR 3 wires per-switch auto-trip + alerting. <!--id:FIX-281-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **DB-backed runtime kill switches + per-request Anthropic spend gate** — Supabase removed the ability to re-enable spend cap after the 8 GB DB upgrade, so we need runtime kill switches that can be flipped without a Vercel redeploy. New `pipeline_state.kill_switches` JSONB row (seeded by [20260517020000](../supabase/migrations/20260517020000_platform_usage_snapshot_and_kill_switches.sql)) holds `{ai_summaries, ai_narrative, ai_tagger, connection_graph_live, cron}`, each `{enabled: bool, auto_trip_threshold_pct: null}`. Helper [packages/db/src/kill-switches.ts](../packages/db/src/kill-switches.ts) exposes `isKillSwitchEnabled(db, name)` / `setKillSwitch` / `clearKillSwitchCache` with layered priority: `env <X> === "false"` (hard kill) > DB switch `enabled=false` > on. DB read miss falls through to "on" — env var is the panic button. 30s module cache bounds staleness; admin flips bust the cache locally. [packages/ai/src/client.ts:185](../packages/ai/src/client.ts#L185) replaces the bare `AI_SUMMARIES_ENABLED` env check with `isKillSwitchEnabled(db, "ai_summaries")` and adds a per-request Anthropic spend ceiling using cached `getMonthlyAnthropicSpend` + `getMonthlyAnthropicLimitUsd` (60s module cache, in [packages/db/src/anthropic-usage.ts](../packages/db/src/anthropic-usage.ts)). New `/api/admin/kill-switches` POST route gated on `ADMIN_EMAIL` via Supabase Auth cookie session — returns 404 on auth fail so the route isn't discoverable. `auto_trip_threshold_pct` is stored but unused in PR 1; PR 3 wires the evaluator + alerting. <!--id:FIX-282-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Live Cloudflare R2 metrics in platform_usage via GraphQL Analytics** — Cloudflare rows in `platform_usage` (`storage_bytes`, `class_a_ops`, `class_b_ops`) sat at manually-entered values from 2026-03-24 (7+ weeks stale) because PR 1's snapshot only knew how to call Supabase + Anthropic. New helper [packages/db/src/cloudflare-usage.ts](../packages/db/src/cloudflare-usage.ts) hits `https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/graphql` once per cycle and queries both `r2StorageAdaptiveGroups` (latest payloadSize + metadataSize per bucket) and `r2OperationsAdaptiveGroups` (MTD requests grouped by `bucketName` × `actionType`) in a single round trip. Operations are classified into Class A (writes — `Put*`, `Copy*`, `List*`, `Create*`, `*MultipartUpload`, `UploadPart*`, `Set*`), Class B (reads — `Get*`, `Head*`, `Usage*`), and free (`Delete*`, `Abort*`); unmapped op types default to Class A (conservative — over-counts writes rather than under-counts for billing alerts). Per-bucket detail returned in `buckets: Record<string, {...}>` so a future per-bucket dashboard tab is unblocked without re-instrumenting; PR 2 only writes the aggregate `totals` to `platform_usage`. 5-min in-memory cache mirrors `getSupabaseManagementMetrics`; missing env returns `{error}` so the snapshot keeps producing rows. New debug route `/api/platform/cloudflare` exposes the raw helper output (GET) and clears the cache (POST + `X-Admin-Key`). Wired into `computePlatformUsagePayload` alongside Supabase. After deploy, all three Cloudflare rows flip from `source='manual'` → `source='api'` on the first cron tick. Token + account ID already in `.env.example`; must be added to Vercel prod env before deploy. PR 2 of 3 (platform monitoring overhaul). <!--id:FIX-283-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Vercel current-cycle usage in platform_usage + env-name fix** — Vercel's nine `platform_usage` rows (`fluid_cpu_seconds`, `function_invocations`, `origin_transfer_bytes`, `edge_requests`, `edge_cpu_ms`, `build_minutes`, `web_analytics_events`, `isr_reads`, `fluid_memory_gb_hrs`) were also stuck on manual values from 2026-03-24. New helper [packages/db/src/vercel-usage.ts](../packages/db/src/vercel-usage.ts) attempts `/v1/usage` first (Pro+ only — Hobby returns `plan_upgrade_required`), then falls back to `/v1/billing/charges` and extracts quantities per `ChargeDescription` using a deterministic substring → metric-key map. Returns a typed `VercelUsage` with every metric defaulted to 0 (downstream `updateUsage()` always has a number to write) plus a `source: "usage" | "charges"` discriminator. Date format requires ISO 8601 with millisecond precision and `Z` suffix — `YYYY-MM-DD` and Unix timestamps are rejected with `invalid_from_date`. 5-min in-memory cache, `{error}` shape on failure. Wired into `computePlatformUsagePayload` and additively merged into `/api/platform/vercel` response under a new `usage` key (existing `metrics`/`total_cost_usd`/`period` consumed by `PlatformCostsSection.tsx` untouched). **Limitation:** site is on Hobby tier today, so both `/v1/usage` and `/v1/billing/charges` ("Plan not found") return errors — Vercel rows will stay manual until Hobby → Pro. The helper ships now so the moment we upgrade, the snapshot self-populates with zero code change. Also fixes the [.env.example](../.env.example) + [turbo.json](../turbo.json) `VERCEL_TOKEN` → `VERCEL_API_TOKEN` name mismatch (route reads `VERCEL_API_TOKEN`; passthrough list was wrong) and adds optional `VERCEL_TEAM_ID`. PR 2 of 3. <!--id:FIX-284-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Kill-switch auto-trip evaluator + per-day overage cadence fix** — PR 3 of 3-PR platform monitoring initiative (PR 1 = FIX-281/282, PR 2 = FIX-283/284, PR 2.5 = FIX-285). The `auto_trip_threshold_pct` field has been stored on every kill_switch since PR 1's seed but never read; this wires it. New migration [20260517040000_kill_switch_metrics_and_cadence.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260517040000_kill_switch_metrics_and_cadence.sql) extends each switch entry with a `metrics: string[]` field listing which `platform_usage` rows can trigger it (default mapping: AI trio → `anthropic.monthly_spend_usd` at 90%, `connection_graph_live` → `supabase.api_requests_7d` + `supabase.disk_used_bytes` at 95%, `cron` → empty array because auto-disabling the cron is destructive — it would stop the very pipeline that detects the auto-trip condition). Also flips `platform_limits.billing_cycle` for `github.storage_bytes` from `monthly_reset` → `per_day_reset` (PR 2.5's caveat) — `calculateOverageCost` now recognizes `per_day_reset` and projects daily cost out to end of current calendar month via `EXTRACT(DAY FROM ...)`-style math (a few cents per month at 1 GiB, not the tiny fraction monthly-cadence math would return). New helper [packages/db/src/auto-trip-evaluator.ts](../packages/db/src/auto-trip-evaluator.ts) runs inside `computePlatformUsagePayload` after all vendor APIs have written through to `platform_usage` but before the snapshot row is inserted. **Freshness gate is mandatory**: auto-trip only fires when `source='api'` AND `recorded_at` within last 30 min (3× the 10-min cron cadence — robust to one missed cycle). Stale-or-manual metrics silently skip with `action: 'skip_stale'` so Vercel's manual 2026-04-22 seed rows don't trigger false positives even though `vercel.origin_transfer_bytes` is otherwise at ~93% of the Hobby limit. **Auto-trip is one-way** — pct dropping back below threshold does NOT flip the switch back on (re-enable is a manual decision via admin POST). Evaluator skips already-off switches to prevent log spam on sustained-overage cycles. Returns full decision list (one entry per switch examined) appended to snapshot payload under `auto_trip_decisions` for cron observability; cron route response also includes `auto_trips_flipped: number` for one-glance log signal. Documented 30s cluster-cache propagation window stays — for non-AI switches it's acceptable; the critical AI path is already short-circuited by PR 1's per-request DB check. <!--id:FIX-286-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **kill_switch_events table + admin dashboard banner** — Companion to FIX-286: auto-trip flips need to surface visibly so they don't happen silently. New migration [20260517050000_kill_switch_events.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260517050000_kill_switch_events.sql) creates `public.kill_switch_events (id, switch_name, trigger_metric, trigger_value, threshold_pct, flipped_to, source CHECK ('auto'|'manual'), flipped_at)` with `flipped_at DESC` index, admin-only RLS (`USING(false)` blocks anon — banner reads via `createAdminClient` service-role on SSR, which bypasses RLS), and `prune_kill_switch_events()` (90-day retention, wired into `runNightlySync` alongside the existing `prune_platform_usage_snapshot` from FIX-281). Refactored [packages/db/src/kill-switches.ts](../packages/db/src/kill-switches.ts) to extract a shared `flipSwitch(db, name, enabled, event)` internal helper used by both `setKillSwitch` (manual, source='manual', no trigger metadata) and `evaluateAutoTrips` (auto, source='auto', carries metric/value/threshold) — avoids double-logging while keeping pipeline_state and kill_switch_events in sync per-flip. `flipSwitch` returns `{event_id, flipped_at}`; the admin POST route now echoes `event_id` so the browser can reflect the new banner row immediately. New `apps/civitics/app/dashboard/KillSwitchBanner.tsx` client component renders `AlertBanner` rows for events in the last hour where `flipped_to=false` (re-enables are non-events). Per-event localStorage dismissal keyed on event id — dismissed events stay dismissed in the same browser; a new flip with a new id reappears even if older events were dismissed. Gated in [dashboard/page.tsx](../apps/civitics/app/dashboard/page.tsx) on `user.email === ADMIN_EMAIL` AND `tab === 'operations'` (cheap server-context user lookup avoids per-pageview kill_switch_events queries for anon traffic). Banner shows above `<DashboardClient />`. <!--id:FIX-287-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Live GitHub Actions usage in platform_usage (enhanced billing API)** — GHA is now part of the operational stack (platform-snapshot every 10 min plus the existing nightly/audit/sync-canary workflows) but no metric was captured for it. Civitics is a public repo so Actions minutes are unlimited and *not* a cost-protection lever today; the work is (a) storage tracking — Actions cache + artifacts ARE billed on public repos above 500 MB, (b) future-proofing — if the repo ever flips private (early-access, sensitive forks) the 10-min platform-snapshot cron alone is ~4,320 billed minutes/month, over double the free 2,000 cap, (c) operational visibility alongside the other vendors. **Two premise corrections from the prompt:** (1) `civitics-platform` is a USER account, not an org (confirmed via `/user` returning login=civitics-platform with no `/user/orgs` membership) — org-level endpoints `/orgs/{org}/settings/billing/*` 404. (2) The legacy user-level `/settings/billing/actions` + `/settings/billing/shared-storage` endpoints return **HTTP 410 "This endpoint has been moved"** as of May 2026 — GitHub sunset both in favor of the unified enhanced-billing endpoint `/users/{user}/settings/billing/usage[?year=YYYY&month=MM]`. Helper [packages/db/src/github-usage.ts](../packages/db/src/github-usage.ts) hits the enhanced endpoint scoped to the current calendar month and aggregates the line-item `usageItems` array (each item has `product`, `sku`, `quantity`, `unitType`, `pricePerUnit`, `grossAmount`, `discountAmount`, `netAmount`). Returns `action_minutes` (sum of `quantity` where `unitType='Minutes'`), `minutes_breakdown` (per-SKU rollup — `{"Actions Linux": 593}` for civitics today; populated automatically if macOS/Windows runners are ever added), `storage_bytes` (best-effort across `product IN ('shared_storage','packages')` — `GigabyteHours` converted to "average bytes over the period so far" via `gbh / hours_elapsed * 1024³`; civitics currently has zero storage line items so this resolves to 0), plus `total_billed_usd` + `total_gross_usd` for completeness. Account path hardcoded as `users/civitics-platform`; swap to `orgs/<slug>` if the repo is ever transferred to a real GitHub Org (endpoint shape is identical). 5-min in-memory cache mirrors Cloudflare/Vercel helpers; failure → `{error}` keeps the snapshot's "data is fresh OR stale, never half-fresh" rule intact. New migration [20260517030000_github_platform_limits.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260517030000_github_platform_limits.sql) seeds two rows: `action_minutes` (plan='free', included_limit=-1 → unlimited per Vercel Pro `function_invocations` pattern at [0024:141](../supabase/migrations/0024_platform_usage_tracking.sql#L141), informational only) and `storage_bytes` (included_limit=500 MB, overage 0.008 per_gb). **Inline-documented caveat:** GitHub charges $0.008/GB/**DAY** for storage — only vendor in the model billed daily not monthly. Downstream overage math treats `overage_unit_cost` as $/(unit) without cadence knowledge, so any future displayed storage overage cost will under-report by ~30× until the evaluator learns about per-day cadence (PR 3 / future FIX). Wired into [computePlatformUsagePayload](../packages/db/src/platform-snapshot.ts) after the Cloudflare branch. New debug route `/api/platform/github` mirrors the Cloudflare shape (GET = raw helper output incl. per-SKU minutes breakdown + USD totals, POST + `X-Admin-Key` = clear cache). New env var `GITHUB_BILLING_TOKEN` (fine-grained PAT owned by `civitics-platform` with account-permission `Plan: Read`; classic PAT with `user` scope also works — existing `GITHUB_TOKEN` has only `read:org, repo`, returns 403 on billing endpoints, confirmed) added to [.env.example](../.env.example) and [turbo.json](../turbo.json) passthrough. Missing token is treated as benign (matches FIX-284 convention — does NOT trip `partial: true`); rows stay on `source='manual'` until token is added. PR 2.5 of 3 (platform monitoring overhaul); per-workflow breakdown + workflow failure rate are explicitly out of scope (would require iterating `/repos/.../actions/runs` — file a follow-up FIX when needed). <!--id:FIX-285-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Email alerts on kill switch flips** — PR 3 (FIX-287) added an admin dashboard banner for `kill_switch_events`, but auto-trip flips at 3 AM stay invisible until someone opens the dashboard. Email is the natural push channel. New `renderKillSwitchEmail()` in [apps/civitics/src/lib/email.ts](../apps/civitics/src/lib/email.ts) renders three subject shapes — `⚠️ AUTO-TRIP: <name> DISABLED` (alarming, with metric/value/threshold in body), `Kill switch disabled: <name>` (manual OFF), `Kill switch re-enabled: <name>` (manual ON, positive tone) — and wraps `renderNotificationEmail` for the HTML shell. Wired into both flip surfaces: cron route iterates `result.payload.auto_trip_decisions.filter(d => d.action === 'flip')` and fires one email per auto-trip; admin POST fires one email per manual flip (both directions, closes the UX gap CC flagged in PR 3 where re-enables had no positive confirmation). New `/api/admin/test-email` endpoint (same admin-email gate as kill-switches POST) sends a hardcoded test email for future "is email working?" sanity checks. Gated on `EMAIL_ALERTS_ENABLED=true` env var (defaults false) plus `ADMIN_EMAIL` plus `RESEND_API_KEY`/`RESEND_FROM`; `sendEmail()` is best-effort and never blocks the flip (matches PR 3 audit-is-secondary posture). No new table, no schema change. Verified end-to-end via synthetic auto-trip (lowered `anthropic.monthly_spend_usd` limit to $0.50 with $1 fake `api_usage_logs` row → 3 flips landed, 3 emails delivered, confirmed via Resend API query). See [[FIX-289]] for Resend operational setup. <!--id:FIX-288-->
