Same secret set now powers [[FIX-288]] kill-switch email alerts, so fix once. Operational, not code: (1) `civitics.com` registered in Resend with verified DKIM/SPF/return-path DNS so `alerts@civitics.com` can send; (2) `RESEND_API_KEY` + `RESEND_FROM` + `EMAIL_ALERTS_ENABLED=true` set in `.env.local` (workspace root AND `apps/civitics/.env.local` — Next.js dev reads the closer scope); (3) same trio added to Vercel prod env (verified via `vercel env ls production`); (4) `RESEND_API_KEY` + `ADMIN_EMAIL` + `RESEND_FROM` added to GHA secrets (`gh secret list` confirms). Also unifies the canary script's hardcoded `alerts@civitics.platform` to read from `RESEND_FROM` env so both surfaces share one From: address. - [x] 🟠 S — **Canary accepts status='partial' + `nightly_killed` marker row** — PR 5 monitoring bundle. The PR 4 canary fired on 2026-05-17 claiming 4 "missing" nightlies; two of the four ([2026-05-13 + 2026-05-16](audits/missing-nightlies-2026-05-10-to-16.md)) were canary false positives — `nightly_cron` rows existed with `status='partial'` and the canary's `.eq("status","complete")` filter excluded them. The other two (5/10 + 5/14, plus 5/17) were real GHA SIGTERMs that wrote no row at all because `runNightlySync` got killed mid-rebuild before reaching its completion-row insert. Fix: (a) [packages/data/src/scripts/canary-check.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/canary-check.ts) now uses `.in("status",["complete","partial"])` because the canary's job is "did it run?" not "did it run cleanly?" (error-tracking is FIX-287's dashboard banner). (b) New [packages/data/src/scripts/mark-killed.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/mark-killed.ts) + `data:mark-killed` / `data:mark-killed:ci` scripts insert a synthetic `pipeline='nightly_killed'` row to `data_sync_log` when the source pipeline has a recent `status='running'` row but no terminal row in the last 4h. Always exits 0 — observability, never a gate. Accepts `--pipeline=<name>` so the same script is reused by FIX-291's rebuild workflow. (c) New `if: always()` post-step in [.github/workflows/nightly.yml](../.github/workflows/nightly.yml) runs the marker after `Run nightly sync` regardless of step outcome. (d) Canary surfaces `nightly_killed` rows with a distinct subject line ("⚠️ Nightly killed by workflow timeout") so "didn't run at all" and "ran but SIGTERM'd" stay separate signals. Pairs with [[FIX-291]] which is the structural fix that makes SIGTERMs rare. <!--id:FIX-290-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Split `rebuild_entity_connections` into its own GHA workflow + raise donations chunk timeout to 90min** — PR 5 structural fix. Cramming the rebuild into the daily nightly was pushing past `timeout-minutes: 120` on 5/14 and 5/17, SIGTERM'd before completion-write. (c) New migration [20260517060000_donations_chunk_timeout_raise.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260517060000_donations_chunk_timeout_raise.sql) raises the donations chunk's function-level `statement_timeout` from 60min → 90min via `ALTER FUNCTION` (cleaner than CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION — body unchanged, just the GUC). Umbrella `rebuild_entity_connections()` preserved for PostgREST local-dev callers. **Trade-off accepted:** `entity_connections` + MVs derived from it (homepage_stats_mv etc.) go stale up to ~3 days between rebuilds. The alternative was the 4-of-7-nights-fail baseline that came from cramming the rebuild into the nightly — 3-day-fresh-but-reliable beats 24h-fresh-but-broken for a transparency platform. Revisit if stale-graph complaints surface. After deploy: `gh workflow run rebuild-entity-connections.yml` was dispatched once to prime prod. <!--id:FIX-291-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Complete Supabase tracking — Pro plan flip + Auth MAU metric** — Two operationally-coupled gaps left by the FIX-281…289 platform-monitoring rollout. (a) `pipeline_state.platform_plan.supabase` was unset on local Docker — without it the dashboard's `getPlatformUsage` returns Free-tier `platform_limits` rows for Supabase, capping `db_size_bytes` at the 500 MB hard limit instead of computing overage against the 8 GB / $0.125/GB Pro pricing. New idempotent script [packages/data/src/scripts/flip-supabase-pro.ts](../packages/data/src/scripts/flip-supabase-pro.ts) (`data:flip-supabase-pro` / `:ci`) reads the existing JSONB, no-ops if `supabase` is already `pro`, otherwise UPSERTs `{supabase: "pro"}` merged with whatever other service overrides are present. Prod already had it set (so the script is local-only this round); checked in for future-proofing. (b) Auth MAUs weren't tracked at all — Supabase Pro includes 100k MAUs and bills $0.00325/MAU above. New migration [20260518000000_supabase_auth_mau.sql](../supabase/migrations/20260518000000_supabase_auth_mau.sql) ships a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC `get_supabase_auth_mau()` that counts `auth.users.last_sign_in_at >= NOW() - INTERVAL '30 days'` (mirrors Supabase's billing-MAU definition) plus `platform_limits` rows for Free (50k hard cap) and Pro (100k included, $0.00325 per overage MAU). New helper `getSupabaseAuthMau()` in [packages/db/src/supabase-usage.ts](../packages/db/src/supabase-usage.ts) wraps the RPC; wired into `computePlatformUsagePayload` alongside the existing Supabase SQL + Management API branches. Failure flagged in the snapshot's `error` field but doesn't trip `partial` (matches the FIX-284 convention — RPC-not-found only happens pre-migration). After deploy the dashboard's Supabase card grows an "Auth MAUs (rolling 30d)" row. <!--id:FIX-295-->

- [x] 🔴 M — **Materialize /api/claude/status/core into a 10-min status_snapshot table** — PR 7 follow-up to PR 6's Step 0 diagnosis. /core was a 9.5 s long pole on prod; `getInitialStatus()` on the dashboard SSR ran the same 11-section parallel block on every pageload. Response shapes unchanged so `useDashboardData` + `DashboardClient` keep working. `prune_status_snapshot` added to the nightly prune list alongside `prune_platform_usage_snapshot` + `prune_kill_switch_events`. - [x] 🔴 M — **Triage 5/19 integrity-audit drift after 5-week blackout** — [[FIX-299]] unblocked the weekly-integrity-audit workflow on 5/19; the first prod report after a 5-week silent-failure window (originally shipped 5/10 by [[FIX-226]]) committed as [docs/audits/2026-05-19.md](audits/2026-05-19.md). Likely fallout from the FEC bulk pipeline's pas2/indiv re-runs that recreated `financial_entities` rows with new UUIDs; old tag rows orphaned. **Likely audit-check bugs (re-validate before treating as real):** (c) `officials.president_count = 2605`, (d) `officials.vp_count = 46`, (e) `officials.senators_per_state` reports `{state: null, count: 100}`, (f) `officials.rep_count = 438` (expected 441). [[FIX-073]]/`be505e79` patched the votes-enum check on 4/19 but did NOT patch the role-title/state checks — those look like they're matching `role_title ILIKE '%president%'` and similar over-broadly, sweeping in former presidents/VPs and candidate rows that joined since the 4/19 snapshot. The senators_per_state NULL row also implies the check isn't filtering on `tier='senator'` or active term-end. Action: (1) read each check in `packages/data/src/pipelines/integrity-audit/checks/officials.ts` against current prod schema (post-2026-04-22 cutover added `tier='candidate'` rows from FIX-246 cn24.zip ingest — probably the noise source); (2) tighten the SQL to active-elected only; (3) file follow-up FIXes per real finding (procedural_contamination and orphan_entity_tags get their own FIX-NNNs once triaged). [agencies/page.tsx:51-67](../apps/civitics/app/agencies/page.tsx#L51-L67) loads up to 200 active agencies and fires two `count:'exact'` queries per agency to compute `totalProposals` + `openProposals` — up to 400 sub-queries hitting `proposals` per pageload, filtered on `metadata->>'agency_id'` (JSONB path is unindexed, so each count is a partial scan). PostgREST silently no-ops the missing-column filter, so `openProposals` likely returns the same number as `totalProposals` rather than open-only. - [x] 🟢 S — **`packages/db` RequestCache tsc errors — recurrence of [[FIX-235]] on 3 sibling usage files** — `pnpm typecheck` fails in `@civitics/db` with 4 × TS2304 `Cannot find name 'RequestCache'`: [cloudflare-usage.ts:189](../packages/db/src/cloudflare-usage.ts#L189), [github-usage.ts:177](../packages/db/src/github-usage.ts#L177), [vercel-usage.ts:239,269](../packages/db/src/vercel-usage.ts#L239). Same shape FIX-235 fixed in `supabase-usage.ts` 5/10: `cache: "no-store"` on a `fetch()` call, but `packages/db/tsconfig.json` correctly omits DOM lib (server-only package), so Node's `RequestInit` stub doesn't declare `cache`. Two later commits (`d46f1f17` Cloudflare R2 + Vercel Usage, `e848f550` GitHub Actions usage) added these sibling files without copying the FIX-235 cast. **Recipe — replicate the FIX-235 fix verbatim per call site:** append `as RequestInit & { cache?: "default" \| "force-cache" \| "no-cache" \| "no-store" \| "only-if-cached" \| "reload" }` to the `fetch()` options object. Do NOT add `"dom"` lib to `packages/db/tsconfig.json` — would pollute downstream consumers that depend on the package being server-only. Doesn't block Vercel (Next's bundler doesn't run `tsc --noEmit`); surfaces only on `pnpm typecheck`. <!--id:FIX-315-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **`packages/blockchain` tsc errors — `TextEncoder` / `crypto` not in scope** — `pnpm typecheck` fails in `@civitics/blockchain` with 2 × TS2304 at [src/index.ts:52-54](../packages/blockchain/src/index.ts#L52-L54): `Cannot find name 'TextEncoder'` and `Cannot find name 'crypto'`. Both are Node globals (since v15 for `crypto`, v11 for `TextEncoder`) but `packages/blockchain/tsconfig.json` lacks the type plumbing to expose them. Two fix shapes: (a) add `"types": ["node"]` to compilerOptions and confirm `@types/node` is a devDependency (preferred — these are genuinely runtime-Node globals); (b) localized `// @ts-expect-error` or `declare const` shims at the call site (avoid — proliferates). Same risk profile as [[FIX-235]]: doesn't block Vercel, only surfaces on `pnpm typecheck`. Verify after the fix that no downstream consumer accidentally gains an implicit `process` / fs / etc. — `@civitics/blockchain` is imported by the client. <!--id:FIX-316-->

Do you need to install type definitions for node? Try 'npm i --save-dev @types/node'`. The error message is the recipe — add `@types/node` as a devDependency and (if not picked up automatically via `typeRoots`) add `"types": ["node"]` to `packages/maps/tsconfig.json`. **Caveat:** `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_MAPBOX_TOKEN` (or similar) is the actual usage at line 15 — confirm it's not a server-only path being read at module load on the client (Next inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` at build, so it's safe even on the client, but worth a glance). Same risk profile as [[FIX-235]] / [[FIX-316]]: doesn't block Vercel, only surfaces on `pnpm typecheck`. <!--id:FIX-317-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Tighten `integrity-audit/checks/officials.ts` to match post-cutover schema (FIX-300 triage)** — [[FIX-300]]'s 4 likely-audit-check findings re-validated against prod data and resolved: (a) `president_count = 2605` — 2603 false positives from FIX-246 cn24.zip `tier='candidate'` rows + 2 randomly-titled federal employees ("SPECIAL REPRESENTATIVE OF THE PRESIDENT", "PRESIDENT, SOUTHWESTERN INDIAN POLYTECHNIC INSTITUTE"); tightened to `tier='elected' AND role_title ~ '^President( of the United States)?$'`. (b) `vp_count = 46` — all 46 are USPS-style executives ("Chief Customer and Marketing Officer and Executive Vice President"); same tightening shape applied for VPOTUS. (c) `senators_per_state` reports `{state: null, count: 100}` — federal senator `metadata` is empty `{}`; state info lives via JOIN `officials.jurisdiction_id → jurisdictions.name`. Rewrote the GROUP BY to use the JOIN; confirmed all 50 states return exactly 2 senators each. (d) `rep_count = 438` — confirmed-real data gap, not an audit-check bug, so check stays as-is and the gap is filed as [[FIX-321]]. The tightened president/VP checks will continue to report 0 vs 1 expected until [[FIX-321]] adds the executive-branch ingest — the values become *correct errors* instead of false-positive ones. Triage doc at [docs/audits/2026-05-19-triage.md](audits/2026-05-19-triage.md). Pattern mirrors [[FIX-073]] (votes-enum check tightening on 4/19, same cycle, same audit pipeline). <!--id:FIX-318-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`referential.orphan_entity_tags = 170` — entity_tags pointing at deleted financial_entities** — Confirmed-real referential-integrity regression surfaced by [[FIX-300]] 5/19 audit. 170 `entity_tags` rows have `(entity_type='financial_entity', entity_id)` that doesn't resolve to any `financial_entities` row. The audit check at [packages/data/src/pipelines/integrity-audit/checks/referential.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/integrity-audit/checks/referential.ts) is correct (LEFT JOIN to verify resolution). Two cleanup paths: (a) delete the 170 orphaned rows — safe if nothing reads `entity_tags` by orphan PK; (b) re-resolve via `donor_fingerprint` UNIQUE on `financial_entities` (added by `20260502120000_financial_entities_donor_fingerprint.sql`) and update the `entity_id` on the tag row to point at the new entity. (b) preserves the human-curated tag attribution; (a) is one DELETE. Decision depends on whether any of the 170 tags carry hand-curated values that the new auto-tagger wouldn't recreate. Also tighten the FEC bulk pipeline so future re-runs don't re-orphan tags — ideally by upserting on `(donor_fingerprint, cycle_year)` rather than letting a fresh UUID get minted. Cross-ref [[FIX-300]] (triage parent), [[FIX-269]] (FEC totals rebuild that may have been the most recent re-mint trigger). <!--id:FIX-320-->

Executive Vice President" pattern), plus appointed roles like "SPECIAL REPRESENTATIVE OF THE PRESIDENT FOR NUCLEAR NONPROLIFERATION" and "PRESIDENT, SOUTHWESTERN INDIAN POLYTECHNIC INSTITUTE". This is observational only, same shape as the FIX-073 / FIX-318 audit additions. <!--id:FIX-322-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Integrity-audit dated-output path collision local-vs-prod** — Surfaced during [[FIX-318]] triage on 2026-05-20. The audit script writes `docs/audits/<YYYY-MM-DD>.{json,md}` regardless of which DB it ran against. Running it locally on the same date as a scheduled prod run produces a path collision — the prod commit and the local run both want the same filenames. Mitigation: when the script runs without `--allow-prod` (i.e., against local Docker), suffix the filename with `-local` (`docs/audits/2026-05-20-local.{json,md}`). Pure script change, no migration, no schema impact. Keeps prod's dated audit chain the canonical artifact while letting local triage runs coexist without colliding. <!--id:FIX-323-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Split `officials.suspicious_elected_tier` true count from sample** — [[FIX-322]] used `ORDER BY full_name LIMIT 20` in the single SQL query, so `actual` reports min(true_count, 20) and we can't tell whether the upstream tier-mapping drift is 20 rows or 200 rows from the audit summary alone. Tiny refactor: split into two queries inside the check — one `SELECT COUNT(*)` for `actual` (unbounded), one `SELECT ... LIMIT 20` for `sample`. Cost: one extra COUNT scan per audit run, negligible. Benefit: drift trend is visible week-over-week in the audit history. Same shape change probably applies to other LIMIT-bound checks in `officials.ts` if any use the same pattern — broaden opportunistically while in the file, but file separately if scope grows. <!--id:FIX-324-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Dashboard /dashboard slow: snapshot staleness hotfix + fallback timeout cap** — Prod `/dashboard` is slow on most loads. Root cause confirmed via direct prod DB inspection 2026-05-22: `status_snapshot` row gaps in the last 10 inserts run 55m / **3h35m** / 1h45m / 1h05m / 1h00m / 1h24m / 1h57m / 1h47m / 2h29m / 2h20m — GHA `*/10 * * * *` is not honored under load (GitHub explicitly warns the schedule event is best-effort). Per-row `query_time_ms` is 30,496–37,135 ms — the live-compute path is dramatically slow on its own. The four staleness constants (3× `SNAPSHOT_STALE_MS = 30 min` + 1× `20 min` in usage route) treat real GHA cadence as failure → dashboard falls back to live recompute on most hits, and the fallback has no upper timeout, so a stale-snapshot hit = 30+ s SSR hang. Fix: bump `SNAPSHOT_STALE_MS` to 4 h (covers 9 of last 10 observed gaps), wrap live-compute fallback in 5 s `Promise.race`, serve last-known-good snapshot on timeout. Hoist both constants to `_lib/status-snapshot.ts` so the four reading sites stay in lockstep. Adds `snapshot_age_minutes` + `served_stale` to `/core` and `/quality` meta envelopes for future banner work. PR 2 ([[FIX-328]]) profiles why `computeStatusPayload` takes 30 s. <!--id:FIX-327-->

- [x] 🟡 S — **Homepage `timed()` helper — drop redundant `console.time` calls causing label-collision warnings under Vercel concurrency** — [apps/civitics/app/page.tsx:288-299](../apps/civitics/app/page.tsx#L288-L299) `timed()` calls `console.time(label)` + `console.timeEnd(label)` while also tracking `ms = Date.now() - t0` into `perf[]` (feeds the hidden `__perf` script payload, the diagnostic surface from [[FIX-223]]). `console.time` labels live on the global `console` object, shared across concurrent invocations in a warm Vercel function instance — so two concurrent `/` renders both racing `home/wave3` collide, producing paired `Label 'home/wave3' already exists` and `No such label 'home/wave3'` warnings on the same render. Keep live-compute fallback via the existing RPC for when the MV row is missing/stale (same pattern as the `homepage_stats_mv` donor-count fallback at `page.tsx:355-...`). The MV row shape lets you drop the JS-side `withDbTimeout` to ~500ms because the MV read is sub-millisecond. **Stopgap option (not part of this FIX):** bump `withDbTimeout(2000)` → `withDbTimeout(5000)` at `page.tsx:526` if the timeout errors are noisy enough in the interim. <!--id:FIX-330-->

- [x] 🟠 S — **Restore `proposals.vote_category` column dropped during shadow→public promotion** — Surfaced as a prerequisite to [[FIX-333]] on 2026-05-23. The 20260422000000 promotion DROPped `public.proposals` and recreated it from `shadow.proposals` — which had never carried `vote_category` — so the column has been silently missing on local since the cutover. Existing `.eq("vote_category", cat)` callers (e.g. `sections.ts:317`) get PostgREST "column not found" errors silently swallowed by `section()` wrappers and return `vote_categories: []`. Also a latent runtime failure path for `compute_alignment_score` (migration `20260425102000`) which references `p.vote_category = 'substantive'` — function exists but would error on first invocation. Fix: new migration `20260523015000_restore_proposals_vote_category.sql` re-applies 0019's shape on `public.proposals` (column + CHECK + index + title-pattern UPDATEs), but uses `type = 'regulation'` for the regulation case since the promoted table doesn't carry `source_ids`. - [x] 🔴 M — **`search_graph_entities('warren')` is a statement-timeout regression — needs trigram indexes** — Surfaced by [[FIX-332]] instrumentation. Direct call `SELECT * FROM public.search_graph_entities('warren', 10)` against prod returns `57014: canceling statement due to statement timeout`; Warren IS in `officials` (single row, Elizabeth Warren / W000817 / Senator) so the timeout is in one of the four UNION ALL branches of the RPC (migration `20260422000007_restore_search_graph_entities.sql`). Strong suspect: the `financial_entities` branch does `display_name ILIKE '%' || q || '%' OR similarity(display_name, q) > 0.3` with no trigram index on `financial_entities.display_name`, and the table is millions of rows. The `proposals` branch's `title ILIKE '%' || q || '%'` is also unindexed and the table is 100k+ rows. Two coupled symptoms: (1) `self_tests:warren_search` floors at ~8.6 s (the service_role statement_timeout cap) on every dashboard cron — the dominant cost inside `self_tests` post-[[FIX-332]] dedup; (2) the `entity_search_finds_warren` self-test fails `passed:false` even though the data is correct — the RPC times out, returns no rows, JS sees Warren "not found". Also a hot path for the graph UI's search-as-you-type. Durable shape: new migration adding GIN trigram indexes — `idx_financial_entities_display_name_trgm USING gin (display_name gin_trgm_ops)` and `idx_proposals_title_trgm USING gin (title gin_trgm_ops)`. Verify with `EXPLAIN ANALYZE search_graph_entities('warren', 10)` — both branches should switch from seq scan to bitmap index scan. Consider dropping the `OR similarity()>0.3` fallbacks once trigram is in (substring search via trigram already covers fuzzy match). <!--id:FIX-335-->

The function's logic only uses `source > 0 && derived === 0` for drift detection — *exact counts are vastly over-spec*; existence is all that matters. Per-rule timing keys removed (always 0 inside one RPC); new `derived_drift:source_presence` key captures the RPC wall-clock. Expected behavior: autovacuum catches up over the next 1-3 days now that the blocking transaction is gone, and visibility-map bloat reduces on its own. Action: re-measure visibility-map state in ~3 days. If still bloated, options are (a) one-off manual `VACUUM (ANALYZE, VERBOSE) financial_relationships` to force catch-up, (b) tighten table-specific autovacuum settings (`autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor` + `autovacuum_analyze_scale_factor` per the FIX-331 pattern that did this for entity_connections), (c) both. Diagnostic to re-run: `SELECT n_live_tup, n_dead_tup, last_autovacuum, last_autoanalyze FROM pg_stat_user_tables WHERE relname = 'financial_relationships';`. Likely self-resolves; this bullet is the tripwire so it doesn't get forgotten. <!--id:FIX-346-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Supabase Prometheus integration + autopopulate egress** — Supabase exposes a Prometheus-compatible endpoint at `https://<ref>.supabase.co/customer/v1/privileged/metrics` (HTTP basic auth, username `service_role`, password is the project's SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY — no new env var). Verified 2026-05-24: 200 OK, ~427 KB body, ~200 series. Egress (`node_network_transmit_bytes_total`) is the one this PR cares about most — `platform_limits.has_public_api` for `(supabase, egress_bytes)` had been `false` since 2026-05-02 (`20260502000000_supabase_live_metrics.sql`, per [[FIX-190]] investigation), and the manual-entry UI for that row was deleted by [[FIX-296]] in PR 1 of this pair leaving the buried PlatformCostsSection Update button as the only fallback. **Shape:** new helper `packages/db/src/supabase-prometheus.ts` mirroring the cloudflare-usage.ts pattern — 5-min in-memory cache, `{error}` on failure, never throws. Counter delta computed app-side via a small `supabase_prometheus_state` table (single row per tracked counter; only egress today): bootstrap writes 0 + captures baseline on first scrape; counter-reset rule (`current < last`) → re-baseline + return 0; monthly rollover rule (baseline_at < `date_trunc('month', NOW())` UTC) → re-baseline + return 0; normal tick returns `current − baseline`. Wired into `computePlatformUsagePayload` after the Cloudflare branch. Migration flips `has_public_api = true` on the egress row so SourceDisplay renders the green "Live" indicator instead of the gray manual treatment. Bootstrap quirk: first scrape after deploy overwrites the stale manual egress value with 0; real delta begins on tick 2. <!--id:FIX-349-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Supabase `db_connections` health metric + move `disk_used_bytes` to Prometheus** — Companion to [[FIX-349]] — the Supabase card was cost-only (db_size_bytes, storage_bytes, disk_used_bytes, api_requests_7d, auth_mau, egress_bytes), no operational signal about pool pressure or slow queries. Adds new `platform_limits` row `(supabase, db_connections, pro)` (Performance display_group, included_limit=60 matching Pro 8GB compute's direct-Postgres backend slots, pure gauge, no overage). Sourced from Prometheus's `pg_stat_database_num_backends`. Same migration: drops the `config/disk/util` call from `getSupabaseManagementMetrics()` (one HTTP round-trip per snapshot saved) — Prometheus's `node_filesystem_{size,avail}_bytes` for the `/data` mount (DB filesystem, 25 GB ext4 on nvme0n1) is the better source and what the Mgmt API was reporting anyway. `SupabaseManagementMetrics.disk_used_bytes` field removed; only `api_requests_7d` remains on that helper. Auto-trip wiring deferred — needs per-metric threshold-and-freshness reasoning per the FIX-286 evaluator pattern; file a follow-up if the gauge turns out to matter operationally. Out of scope for this PR: per-bucket egress drill-down, `pg_stat_statements` slow-log surfacing, replication-lag / pgbouncer-pool metrics. <!--id:FIX-350-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Supabase disk-utilization % uses provisioned size, not plan-included quota** — Hotfix to [[FIX-349]] / [[FIX-350]]. Dashboard showed Disk Utilization `13.5 GB / 8 GB = 169%` because `platform_limits.included_limit` for `(supabase, disk_used_bytes, pro)` was seeded as 8 GB — the Pro plan included quota (correct for `db_size_bytes` billing). The denominator for *disk utilization* should be the provisioned filesystem size (currently 24 GB after a manual upgrade), not the included quota. Prometheus already exposes `node_filesystem_size_bytes{mountpoint="/data"}` as part of the FIX-349 helper. **Shape:** expose `disk_size_bytes` on `SupabasePrometheusMetrics` and inside `computePlatformUsagePayload`'s Prometheus success branch, UPDATE the single `(supabase, disk_used_bytes, pro)` row in `platform_limits` with `included_limit = metrics.disk_size_bytes` and a refreshed `notes` string each tick. No new migration — the seed migration stays append-only; first snapshot tick after deploy overwrites the value dynamically. Idempotent. Failure-mode: on Prometheus error the limit stays at last-known via the existing `{error}` short-circuit. Documents the dynamic-limit deviation inline at the UPDATE call site since every other `platform_limits` row remains config-shaped. <!--id:FIX-351-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`platform_limits.display_limit` column — separate billing-denominator from capacity-bar denominator** — Follow-up to [[FIX-349]] / [[FIX-350]] / [[FIX-351]]. `supabase.db_size_bytes` displays `~13 GB / 8 GB = 169%` red bar but the 8 GB IS the correct Pro plan included quota for billing — overage cost math ($0.125/GB over 8 GB = $0.625/mo at 13 GB) is right. The bar % is wrong because capacity-denominator (provisioned disk = ~24 GB) and billing-denominator (8 GB included quota) legitimately differ. **Shape:** new nullable column `platform_limits.display_limit BIGINT` (migration `20260524200436_platform_limits_display_limit.sql`); new exported helper `effectiveLimit(row) = row.display_limit ?? row.included_limit`; `getPlatformUsage`'s `pct` calculation uses `effectiveLimit()`, `calculateOverageCost` continues to use `included_limit` directly. Snapshot job in `computePlatformUsagePayload`'s Prometheus success branch UPDATEs `(supabase, db_size_bytes, pro).display_limit = metrics.disk_size_bytes` each tick (same value FIX-351 already reads). `disk_used_bytes` continues to use the FIX-351 `included_limit` override pattern (disk has no billing-overage, so its single denominator is fine). `effectiveLimit` is forward-compatible for any future metric where the same separation matters (e.g. Vercel function invocations, GitHub Actions minutes). Mirrors the type changes in `client-safe.ts`. No UI changes — `MetricRow` already renders `metric.pct`. <!--id:FIX-353-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **`db_connections` pool max — dynamic per compute add-on tier from Management API** — Hotfix to [[FIX-350]]. `platform_limits.included_limit` for `(supabase, db_connections, pro)` was seeded as 60, matching the direct-Postgres pool size for the **large** (8 GB) compute add-on tier. Wrong for projects on micro/small/medium (15) or xl+ (120+); silently divergent if the project is upgraded. **Shape:** new helper `getSupabaseComputeTier()` in [packages/db/src/supabase-usage.ts](../packages/db/src/supabase-usage.ts) hitting `/v1/projects/{ref}/billing/addons` Management API and parsing `selected_addons[].variant.identifier === 'ci_<tier>'`; falls back to `"micro"` when no compute_instance add-on is selected (default Pro compute). 1-hour in-memory cache (tier changes are rare). Module-level `SUPABASE_COMPUTE_POOL_MAX` lookup table + `poolMaxForTier(tier)` accessor. Snapshot job in `computePlatformUsagePayload` UPDATEs `(supabase, db_connections, pro).included_limit = poolMaxForTier(tierResult.tier)` each tick. On Management API failure, the helper returns `{error}` and `poolMaxForTier(undefined)` falls back to `60` — matches the prior static value, no regression. **Verify table values against Supabase docs when adding new tiers** — pool max numbers shift occasionally on Supabase's side. Debug route at `/api/platform/supabase-prometheus` includes the resolved tier + pool max in its GET response so the value is grep-able from Vercel logs. <!--id:FIX-354-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Supabase CPU % tracking — current snapshot from Prometheus counter deltas** — Companion to [[FIX-349]] / [[FIX-350]]. Supabase card has disk + connections but no CPU signal — "is the box hot right now?" isn't observable from the dashboard, only from the Supabase project console. Prometheus exposes `node_cpu_seconds_total{cpu="<n>",mode="<idle|user|system|iowait|...>"}` as a per-core monotonic counter. **Shape:** new `applyTickDelta()` helper in [packages/db/src/supabase-prometheus.ts](../packages/db/src/supabase-prometheus.ts) — same `supabase_prometheus_state` table FIX-349 introduced, but returns `current - last_raw` (per-tick delta) instead of `current - baseline` (month-to-date) since CPU has no monthly accumulator semantic. Counter-reset detection (`current < last`) on node restart sets both fields to current + returns 0, avoiding wild ticks. Helper walks every `node_cpu_seconds_total{...}` line, accumulates `busy_delta` (sum where `mode != 'idle'`) and `total_delta` (sum over all modes + cores), computes `cpu_pct = 100 * busy_delta / total_delta` — num_cores divides out of both so the formula is robust across compute-tier upgrades. Two new state rows seeded on first tick (`cpu_busy_seconds_total`, `cpu_total_seconds_total`). New `platform_limits` row `(supabase, cpu_pct, pro)` with Performance display_group, included_limit=100, no overage. Migration `20260524210000_supabase_cpu_tracking.sql`. Auto-trip wiring deliberately deferred — sustained-vs-instantaneous threshold reasoning per the FIX-286 evaluator pattern is its own design problem. <!--id:FIX-355-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Use Postgres `max_connections` (not PgBouncer pool size) for `db_connections` limit** — Corrects [[FIX-354]] / [[FIX-350]]. FIX-354 set `platform_limits.included_limit` for `(supabase, db_connections, pro)` from a tier→pool-size lookup keyed off the Management API's compute-tier identifier. Wrong axis: `pg_stat_database_num_backends` (the gauge) counts **Postgres backends** bounded by `max_connections`, not PgBouncer client connections bounded by `default_pool_size`. The hardcoded 15 for Micro produced a misleading `18 / 15 = 120%` red bar even though Postgres's actual `max_connections = 60` cap was nowhere close. **Shape:** new SECURITY DEFINER RPC `get_supabase_max_connections() RETURNS INT` that returns `current_setting('max_connections')::INT` (STABLE, `SET search_path = public`, GRANT to authenticated + service_role — mirrors `get_supabase_auth_mau` from FIX-295). Snapshot job calls the RPC and writes through to `platform_limits.included_limit` for the `db_connections` row. Self-correcting on tier upgrades (Supabase adjusts `max_connections` automatically), no external API call, no lookup table to maintain. **Deletions:** `getSupabaseComputeTier()` + module cache, `clearSupabaseComputeTierCache()`, `SUPABASE_COMPUTE_POOL_MAX` constant, `poolMaxForTier()`, related types — plus their re-exports from `packages/db/src/index.ts` and the `compute_tier` / `pool_max` fields on the `/api/platform/supabase-prometheus` debug response. Net code reduction. Fallback: on RPC error the UPDATE is skipped, the value stays last-known-good. <!--id:FIX-357-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Supabase CPU max-1h + max-24h windowed maxima** — Companion to [[FIX-355]]. Current % alone doesn't answer "did the box peak overnight?" — needs a recent-history view. **Shape:** new `get_supabase_cpu_max(window_minutes INT)` RPC reads `payload->'supabase_cpu'->>'current_pct'` from `platform_usage_snapshot` rows in the window, returns MAX(). SECURITY DEFINER with `statement_timeout = '5s'` per [[reference_function_statement_timeout]]. Helper calls RPC twice (60 min and 1440 min) and folds in the current tick's value via `Math.max(rpc_result, cpu_pct_current)` — current tick hasn't been written yet at compute time. Fail-soft: RPC error keeps `cpu_max_* = cpu_pct_current` so the dashboard still renders. New optional top-level `supabase_cpu` field on `PlatformUsagePayload` stores all three values (current/1h/24h) at a stable path the RPC can fish out. Same numbers stitched onto the cpu_pct PlatformMetric's new optional `metadata` field so the dashboard renders `max 1h: X% · 24h: Y%` as a sub-label under the standard MetricRow value line. Same migration as [[FIX-355]]. Out of scope: max-7d (different snapshot retention math; current prune is 30 d but the RPC's window math gets clumsy past a few hours), per-mode breakdown (iowait/steal). <!--id:FIX-356-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **FIXES.md/done.log CRLF tolerance + explicit .gitattributes rules** — Hardens the FIXES.md/done.log sync against CRLF reads. The root .gitattributes already had a broad `* text=auto eol=lf` rule, but a Windows editor saving CRLF in the working tree (uncommitted) would let `pnpm fixes:sync` silently no-op — its bullet/marker regex is `\n`-anchored and treats `\r` as part of the previous segment. Adds explicit `docs/FIXES.md text eol=lf` and `docs/done.log text eol=lf` lines (documentation + force text classification on top of the broad auto-detect) and tolerates CRLF on all read sites in `scripts/fixes-sync.mjs`, `scripts/fixes-clean.mjs`, `scripts/fixes-archive.mjs`, and `scripts/fixes-housekeep.mjs` via `.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n')`. Writes stay LF unconditionally via `.join('\n')`. Cross-ref [[FIX-363]] which depends on this normalizer for its own read path. <!--id:FIX-361-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`pnpm fix:add` script — centralized FIX bullet creation + atomic ID allocation** — Adds `scripts/fix-add.mjs` (wired as `pnpm fix:add` in root `package.json`) so both Craig and Claude Code call the same script when appending new FIX bullets. Replaces the per-prompt `grep -oE 'FIX-[0-9]+' docs/FIXES.md | sort -u | tail -5` + hand-format-bullet recipe that has caused inconsistent bullet shapes and the occasional FIX-ID collision. Args interface: `--title` / `--severity` / `--size` / `--section` / `--body`. Allocator mirrors `scripts/fixes-housekeep.mjs` (max across FIXES.md + done.log + archive + 1). Concurrency model is optimistic-with-one-retry: read → hash → format → re-read → write iff hash unchanged; bails on second collision. Atomic write via tmp + rename. Insertion point is after the last bullet in the matched section, before the next `^## ` header. CLAUDE.md FIXES Workflow section updated with the additive-only contract + script usage. Depends on [[FIX-361]] for CRLF-safe reads. Out of scope (deferred until needed): `$EDITOR` interactive mode; `fix:close` / `fix:reopen` companions (existing trailer/`fixes:sync` flow handles closure); proper-lockfile (optimistic retry is enough at solo-dev scale). <!--id:FIX-363-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **fix:add allocator counts markers only, not cross-refs or prose** — scripts/fix-add.mjs allocator's grep was `/FIX-\d{3}/g` — counted [[FIX-NNN]] cross-refs and prose mentions, not just real allocations. - [x] 🟡 S — **withDbTimeout surfaces PostgREST structural errors** — The withDbTimeout wrapper at apps/civitics/src/lib/supabase-check.ts faithfully preserves {data, error} from the Supabase client, but every call site destructures { data } only — error is discarded silently. PostgREST returns 42703 column does not exist, 42P01 table does not exist, etc. as {data: null, error: {code, message}}, so a route that selects a dropped column returns 0 rows with no log line. This is the structural reason [[FIX-308]] (OfficialCard $0), [[FIX-334]] (vote_category MIA), [[FIX-344]] (summary route donor count $0), and [[FIX-374]] (sunburst donor wedge empty) all hid for weeks-to-months post-2026-04-22 cutover. Fix: console.error PostgREST structural errors (codes 42703/42P01/42883/42704/42P02) inside the wrapper before returning. Data path + return shape unchanged; pure additive observability. <!--id:FIX-378-->

Validate against any new tables added after 2026-05-25. Cross-ref [[FIX-271]] (template), [[FIX-320]] (cleanup), [[FIX-245]] (surfacing case). <!--id:FIX-381-->

- [x] 🟡 S — **Congress officials xsr seed — backfill external_source_refs from source_ids.congress_gov + wire congress/officials.ts** — [[FIX-397]] deploy report (2026-05-26) flagged officials xsr binding rate at 33% on prod (9,434 / 28,734). Root cause: packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/officials.ts uses the legacy source_ids JSONB pattern — writes source_ids: { congress_gov: bioguideId } directly on the officials row and never inserts into external_source_refs. The ~19,300 unbound officials are mostly current + historical Congress members from this writer (sitting members + retired/former + candidates from past cycles). Action: (a) one-shot seed migration — INSERT INTO external_source_refs (source, external_id, entity_type, entity_id, source_url, last_seen_at, metadata) SELECT 'congress_gov', source_ids->>'congress_gov', 'official', id, 'https://www.congress.gov/member/' || (source_ids->>'congress_gov'), COALESCE(updated_at, NOW()), '{}'::jsonb FROM officials WHERE source_ids ? 'congress_gov' AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM external_source_refs xsr WHERE xsr.source='congress_gov' AND xsr.external_id = officials.source_ids->>'congress_gov'). ON CONFLICT (source, external_id) DO NOTHING for safety. (b) Update congress/officials.ts to call refreshPrimarySourceForEntities + write xsr alongside the source_ids JSONB stamp on every member upsert (don't remove source_ids yet — that's the broader source_ids → xsr migration residue, not in scope here). (c) After seed migration, run rebuild_all_primary_sources() once to materialize primary_source on the freshly-bound officials. Expected lift: officials primary_source binding rate 33% → ~97% on prod. This is the partner FIX to [[FIX-397]] for officials specifically — the same residue exists for other pre-cutover writers but officials is the highest-visibility surface (federal members of Congress are the most-viewed entities). Cross-ref [[FIX-397]] (the column this lifts data for), [[FIX-251]] (xsr canonical binding pattern that this completes for congress/officials). <!--id:FIX-403-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Wire refreshPrimarySourceForEntities into remaining 5 xsr-writing pipelines** — [[FIX-397]] shipped the writer-side helper refreshPrimarySourceForEntities (packages/db/src/primary-source.ts) and integrated it into 4 xsr-writing pipelines (littlesis, irs990, usaspending-bulk, congress/bills.ts). 5 other xsr-writing pipelines were not in scope for that PR and currently rely on the nightly rebuild_all_primary_sources() safety net — meaning new data from these pipelines has up to a 24h lag in primary_source attribution visibility. Each integration is a 1-3 line addition mirroring the FIX-397 pattern in littlesis/writer.ts — after the xsr upsert batch completes, collect the affected (entity_type, entity_id[]) pairs and call refreshPrimarySourceForEntities once per entity_type group. Closes the 24h lag for ~80% of new xsr writes; nightly safety net catches the remaining edge cases. Cross-ref [[FIX-397]] (the helper this propagates), [[FIX-402]] (governing_bodies dependency for legistar integration). <!--id:FIX-404-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **FEC synthetic attribution — synthesize primary_source from fec_committee_id at derivation time** — [[FIX-397]] deploy report (2026-05-26) confirmed financial_entities primary_source binding rate at 3.7% on prod (89,701 / 2,416,197). Root cause: FEC dedup is structurally outside the xsr pattern — fec-bulk/writer.ts uses financial_entities.fec_committee_id UNIQUE for canonical dedup and never writes to external_source_refs. ~2.3M financial_entities rows have NULL primary_source today. Agreed path: synthetic derivation, NOT xsr seed (xsr seed would inflate xsr by 1.7M rows on a Small-tier IOWait-bound Postgres — index size, scan time, vacuum cost all degrade). Principle: xsr is the home for sources where multiple bindings per entity are realistic (senator with congress_gov + openstates; nonprofit with irs_990 + littlesis + usaspending); first-class binding columns like fec_committee_id mean the source IS the canonical identity (one FEC committee ID = one FEC committee deterministically) and M2M overhead is wasted — synthesize attribution from the column at derivation time. Action: (a) extend refresh_primary_source_for_entities() SQL function to add a CASE branch when entity_type='financial_entity' AND fec_committee_id IS NOT NULL — synthesize primary_source='fec', primary_source_url='https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/' || fec_committee_id || '/', primary_source_last_seen_at=COALESCE(updated_at, NOW()). Use ONLY when no xsr row exists (real xsr binding always wins). (b) extend /api/attribution/[type]/[id] route (FIX-398's new route) to prepend a synthetic {source: 'fec', external_id: fec_committee_id, is_primary, priority: source_priority('fec'), source_url: derived, last_seen_at: entity.updated_at} entry to the sources array when financial_entity has fec_committee_id. (c) add helper synthesizeAttributionFromFirstClass(entity) in packages/db/src/attribution.ts shared between API route + SQL derivation conceptually (URL template duplicated in SQL since SQL can't import TS; document the duplication with a comment cross-ref). (d) after migration, run rebuild_all_primary_sources() once to backfill financial_entities synthesis. Expected lift: financial_entities primary_source binding rate 3.7% → ~75% on prod (the portion of rows with fec_committee_id IS NOT NULL). Cross-ref [[FIX-397]] (the materialization layer this extends), [[FIX-398]] (the API route this extends). Future first-class binding columns (e.g., congress_gov in source_ids → already getting xsr-seed treatment via [[FIX-403]], so not synthesis) follow the same pattern when row count exceeds xsr-seed comfort (~100K rows is the rough cutoff between "just seed" and "synthesize"). <!--id:FIX-406-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Add public-read RLS policy to external_source_refs — eliminate createAdminClient dance** — [[FIX-398]] deploy report (2026-05-26) surfaced that external_source_refs (xsr) has RLS enabled with zero policies — service-role only. SSR data fetches using the publishable-key client silently return source_count: 0 (RLS denies cleanly with no error; PostgREST returns data:[], error:null). FIX-398 workaround: lazily instantiate createAdminClient for the count subquery in fetchAttributionForEntity, which works but adds a two-client dance to every SSR attribution read and will re-bite every future SSR route that needs xsr data. xsr columns are pure attribution metadata (source, external_id, entity_type, entity_id, source_url, last_seen_at, metadata) — none of it is sensitive (it's the exact data we're about to render publicly via [[FIX-399]] / [[FIX-400]]). Action: one-line migration adding a public-read RLS policy: CREATE POLICY xsr_public_read ON public.external_source_refs FOR SELECT TO anon, authenticated USING (true). Service-role writes remain unaffected (service_role bypasses RLS). Once landed, simplify fetchAttributionForEntity in packages/db/src/attribution.ts to drop the createAdminClient instantiation — single publishable client handles both the entity-table read and the xsr count. The metadata JSONB is a slight wildcard (some pipelines may stash provenance hints there); if anything sensitive lives there today, audit + scrub before this lands. Cross-ref [[FIX-398]] (the workaround this eliminates), [[FIX-397]] (the schema that established the no-policy state). <!--id:FIX-408-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **@civitics/data tsc fails on three pre-existing files — blocks pipeline smoke verification** — Surfaced during [[FIX-404]] verification 2026-05-27. `pnpm --filter @civitics/data build` does not pass clean — three pre-existing tsc errors in unrelated files block every writer/pipeline-touching FIX from recording `Verified: local + prod` because the broken build prevents `pnpm data:<pipeline> --max=N` smoke runs. CC has had to caveat around this on multiple recent prompts (the workaround on [[FIX-414]] is direct-SQL fallback; on [[FIX-404]] it was build-only + reasoning, dropping the trailer to `Verified: local`). The three offenders (investigate before fixing — bullet diagnoses are author-best-guess): (a) `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/writer.ts:559` — relationship_type widening error (likely a string-literal narrowing issue; check whether destination type wants a union or the source needs `as const`); (b) `packages/data/src/scripts/attribution-binding-rate.ts` — cannot find module `@supabase/supabase-js` (the script imports it but the package isn't declared in `packages/data/package.json` devDependencies; relying on pnpm hoisting at runtime, which tsc rejects); (c) `packages/data/src/scripts/backfill-individual-canonical-name.ts` — implicit-any (likely a function parameter or destructured value missing annotation). All three are surgical. Goal: `pnpm --filter @civitics/data build` exits 0 so future writer-side FIXes can record `Verified: local + prod` via actual pipeline runs instead of build-only reasoning. Cross-ref [[FIX-404]], [[FIX-414]]. <!--id:FIX-417-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Wire @civitics/data into turbo typecheck gate** — @civitics/data had no typecheck script so turbo run typecheck skipped it — root cause behind [[FIX-417]]. Adds tsc --noEmit to gate the package going forward. <!--id:FIX-423-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Financial-entity size tagging OOM'd prod — move donation rollup server-side** — FIX-437's get_financial_entity_donation_totals() returns one jsonb_agg array over every donor entity (derived from prod's 4.88M financial_relationships donation rows). Materializing that single huge Datum + buffering it through PostgREST OOM'd the prod Pro instance (postmaster restart 2026-05-30 20:52:17 UTC, FATAL 57P03 in logs). The client-side size-tag build is the hazard; the NAICS-industry path is ~78 entities and the keyword-industry path is already paginated. Fix: server-side rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags() (DELETE + INSERT..SELECT the 4 size buckets from the donation aggregate, returns a count, mirrors rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags) + a tiny get_financial_entity_naics() RPC (~78 rows) to feed the unchanged JS NAICS loop. rules.ts tagFinancialEntities: drop the rollup fetch + size loop + combined size+industry clear; keep keyword+NAICS JS dedup intact; clear only 'industry' in JS. Net: no large array ever crosses the gateway. Follow-up to [[FIX-437]]. <!--id:FIX-443-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Tagger heavy rebuild RPCs must use a direct pg connection on prod (gateway times out + retries pile up)** — rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags() (FIX-443) inserts prod's full donor set — ~2.27M rows, >2min — which the ~100s PostgREST/Cloudflare gateway cannot run; worse, callWithRetry fires 5 retries while the original is still running server-side, piling up concurrent 2-min functions that lock-contend on entity_tags (observed 2026-05-30). Postgres also arms statement_timeout at the top-level statement using the SESSION value, so the function-level ALTER SET statement_timeout='300s' does NOT extend the current call — the prod session default (~120s) wins. Same applies to rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags (80s on prod). Durable fix: route these heavy rebuild RPCs through a direct pg.Client (SUPABASE_DB_URL session pooler) with statement_timeout raised at session level, exactly like data:rebuild-connections:ci does — NOT db.rpc() over PostgREST. Until then the nightly tagger fails at the financial-size + pre_vote steps on prod (tags don't refresh; manually-landed size 2.27M / internal 461k persist since failed rebuilds roll back). Also unblocks the still-stale industry category (~904, should be ~tens of thousands) which needs the pipeline's JS keyword pass to run end-to-end on prod. Tonight size+internal were landed manually via direct psql + statement_timeout=0. Follow-up to [[FIX-443]] / [[FIX-437]]. <!--id:FIX-444-->

### COMMUNITY & AUTH

- [x] 🟡 M — **Role/claim spine — entity_grants/evidence/events schema (v1: constituent + platform_admin)** — Lands the schema for the platform identity layer: entity_grants, grant_evidence, grant_events, plus has_active_constituent_grant() helper. v1 enums limited to 'constituent' + 'platform_admin'; 'verified_human', 'official', 'staff', 'jurisdiction_admin', 'institution_admin' deferred to v2 (pure ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE). No consumer UI/route in this FIX — first user is the Mapbox-backed constituent verification flow (separate FIX). All writes go through service-role until v2 feature consumers land. <!--id:FIX-419-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Mapbox-backed constituent verification flow — first consumer of FIX-419 grants** — Adds /api/auth/verify-constituent (POST) and a Verify-as-Constituent form on /profile. Server-side Mapbox v6 forward geocode → ST_Contains spatial lookup against jurisdictions.boundary_geometry (RPC jurisdictions_containing_point) → one entity_grants row per matched jurisdiction (role=constituent, status=active, expires_at=NOW()+2y) plus a grant_evidence row and grant_events 'auto_approved' entries. <!--id:FIX-420-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Jurisdiction boundary backfill — states + cities (+ DC/territories) from Census TIGER 2024** — Populates jurisdictions.boundary_geometry + centroid for state / county / place rows from Census TIGER 2024 shapefiles — the levels [[FIX-420]] constituent verification needs to grant beyond districts. New pipeline packages/data/src/pipelines/jurisdictions-boundary-backfill (run: `pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:boundaries -- --levels state,place [--state-fips=53,06,36,48] [--allow-prod] [--dry-run]`). New SECURITY DEFINER RPC backfill_jurisdiction_boundary(p_id,p_geojson) in migration 20260528170000 (search_path=public,extensions per [[FIX-420]] PostGIS-in-extensions gotcha; ST_Multi(ST_GeomFromGeoJSON)→MULTIPOLYGON, centroid via ST_PointOnSurface, UPDATE-only WHERE boundary_geometry IS NULL — idempotent, never overwrites, never inserts). upsert_district_jurisdiction ([[FIX-160]]) could NOT be reused: it INSERTs-on-no-match and hardcodes type=district, which would mint forbidden new rows. <!--id:FIX-421-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Jurisdictions follow-ups bundle — county creation + centroid backfill + DC/territory type correction** — Three coherent jurisdictions-table follow-ups in one FIX, building on [[FIX-421]]. (1) Adds jurisdiction_type values federal_district + unincorporated_territory (migration 20260528180000, isolated ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE — can't be used in the same txn it's added) and re-types DC (1 row → federal_district) and PR/VI/GU/AS/MP (5 rows → unincorporated_territory) off the catch-all 'district' bucket (migration 20260528180100), guarded by census_geoid IS NULL so real legislative/congressional districts can't match. (2) Centroid backfill via ST_PointOnSurface for the ~6,775 pre-existing district rows that had a boundary but no centroid ([[FIX-421]]'s RPC only sets centroid on rows it backfills). (3) County row creation: new SECURITY DEFINER RPC upsert_county_jurisdiction (migration 20260528180200, search_path=public,extensions, insert-on-no-match + COALESCE-fill so it never overwrites an existing boundary) + extension of [[FIX-421]]'s data:boundaries --levels county from a no-op into a real create+backfill pass. 3,235 county rows inserted with parent_id resolved by STATEFP (incl. 78 PR municipios + AS/GU/MP/VI/DC county-equivalents under the re-typed parents — proves the re-type ordering), boundary+centroid populated in the same upsert. Run: pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:boundaries -- --levels county [--dry-run] [--state-fips=53,06,36]. After this lands, /api/auth/verify-constituent ([[FIX-420]]) grants at county level (King County for a Seattle address). Also hardened [[FIX-421]]'s loadSnapshot: it silently swallowed query errors, so a transient PostgREST 'fetch failed' on the states query produced mass no-parent skips that looked like a clean run — now throws; and the counties snapshot query now paginates past the 1,000-row PostgREST cap. NOTE: local DB has no CD119 congressional districts loaded (annual/manual districts-tiger pass), so local spatial smoke shows 5 grants (state, city, SLDU, SLDL, +county); prod should show 6 if CD119 is present. City-row creation beyond the 4 pilot metros remains a documented follow-up. <!--id:FIX-422-->
- [x] 🟡 L — **/jurisdictions/[id] page v1 — full hub with static SVG map** — Ships the jurisdiction-level landing page. UUID-canonical route (/jurisdictions/<uuid>; non-UUID → notFound, no slug branch in v1). ISR (revalidate=300) statically renderable; generateStaticParams warms the federal jurisdiction + 50 states. Type-conditional sections that omit when empty: header (type badge + parent breadcrumb + population/timezone/fips + FollowButton), server-rendered static SVG map (new jurisdiction_boundary_svg RPC via ST_AsSVG — no Mapbox JS), Verify-as-Constituent surface (client island hitting new /api/constituent-status, reuses [[FIX-420]] VerifyConstituentForm), child-jurisdictions nav, institutions list, officials roster, recent proposals, upcoming/recent meetings, civic initiatives, spending overview, and a recent-activity feed (new get_jurisdiction_activity RPC). Both RPCs SECURITY DEFINER + search_path=public,extensions + statement_timeout, granted anon/authenticated/service_role. Extends follow_entity_type enum with 'jurisdiction' (+ widens /api/follows + FollowButton). New shared cards in app/components/cards/ (InstitutionCard, MeetingCard, OfficialRosterCard); reuses existing ProposalCard + InitiativeCard cross-route. SCHEMA-REALITY CORRECTIONS vs the spec: live DB has no civic_initiatives table (initiatives are proposals type='initiative' + initiative_details) and no spending_records table (spending is financial_relationships sourced from agencies in the jurisdiction) and no primary_source* columns on jurisdictions (SourceBadge omitted). Known follow-ups: /meetings/[id] detail route doesn't exist (MeetingCard link is a documented dead-end); WA boundary SVG path is ~180KB (perf-optimize precision later); /jurisdictions index + slug routing out of scope. Builds on [[FIX-418]] institutions view, [[FIX-419]] grants, [[FIX-420]] constituent verify, [[FIX-421]]/[[FIX-422]] boundary coverage. <!--id:FIX-424-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Polish bundle — /jurisdictions/[id] SVG simplification + notFound middleware** — Two follow-ups to [[FIX-424]]. (1) jurisdiction_boundary_svg RPC now applies ST_SimplifyPreserveTopology with type-adaptive tolerance (area>1→0.005, >0.1→0.001, >0.01→0.0005, else→0.0001) + precision-4 output. Measured local before/after (raw p6 → simplified p4): FL 441KB→7.2KB, TX 1.4MB→26.8KB, CA 276KB→9.8KB, WA 181KB→3.4KB (this is the ~180KB path FIX-424 flagged), Seattle 23KB→2.2KB, King County 266KB→1.9KB, RI-state-house-district-56 →343B. All states <50KB, counties/cities <10KB target met; thin small districts retain 18–89 pts via PreserveTopology (no collapse). FL/CA/WA outlines render cleanly at 480px wide. (2) Middleware UUID-format guard for /jurisdictions/<id> returns a true HTTP 404 for malformed paths instead of page-level notFound() degrading to 200 under loading.tsx Suspense (same root cause as [[FIX-418]]'s /agencies 308). Shared UUID_RE const extracted across both branches; existing broad :path* matcher already covers /jurisdictions. Valid-UUID-no-row case still returns 200 by design (edge middleware can't do DB lookups). Known follow-ups: same notFound()-under-Suspense gotcha exists on /districts/[id], /institutions/[id], /officials/[id], /proposals/[id] (all have loading.tsx + notFound()) — not fixed here. Pre-computed SVG cache column deferred. <!--id:FIX-433-->

- [x] 🟡 M — **Clean up procedural-vote / court-case rows already in `public.proposals`** — FIX-072 fixed the contamination *path* via the shadow→public promotion (vote_question is now first-class on `votes`, `bill_details`/`case_details` separate the row types) but the bad rows that landed before the cutover are still there. Findings: ~169 titles matching `^on ` (procedural vote questions — see CLAUDE.md §votes) leaked from the votes ingester; ~320 titles matching ` v. ` (court case names) leaked from a SCOTUS/courts docket pipeline; both groups have `summary_plain = NULL`, `metadata->>'agency_id' = NULL`; most contamination sits under `type = 'other'`. Decide quarantine destination (move to a `proposals_archive`, or set `metadata.contaminated=true` and exclude from queries) before deleting. <!--id:FIX-162-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Re-title 147 amendment-format Senate vote stubs in local `public.proposals`** — Follow-up to FIX-162. The 147 stubs the FIX-162 retitler skipped have `legacy_bill_number` like `S 11-22`, `S 345-15` — `S {parent_bill_number}-{amendment_number}`, not a regular bill identifier. They anchor 29,698 votes via `votes.bill_proposal_id`, so deletion is off the table. Approaches: (a) call Congress.gov `amendment/{congress}/samdt/{number}` (or `suamdt`) to fetch the amendment description; (b) fetch the parent bill `S {N}` and prefix the title with `Amendment to:`; (c) format placeholder title `Senate Amendment {M} to S {N} (119th Congress)` with no API call. Whichever is chosen, extend `parseBillNumber` in `packages/data/src/scripts/retitle-procedural-bill-stubs.ts` to recognise the amendment shape and dispatch to the right path. Prod has zero rows in this state — local-only fix. <!--id:FIX-164-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Pipeline fix: handle Presidential Nominations (PN) in Senate votes ingester** — `normalizeSenateDocType` in [packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts) returns `S` for any unrecognized `<document_type>`, including PN. Result: Senate confirmation votes land in `proposals` with the procedural question as title, `type=bill` instead of `appointment`, and a fake `legacy_bill_number` like `S 11-22`. FIX-162 + FIX-164 cleaned up the historical mess; this item is the upstream fix so the next vote pipeline run doesn't re-introduce it. Approach: detect `document_type='PN'` in [packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts) (around line 696), build a separate proposal record with `type='appointment'`, title from the XML's `<document_title>`, `legacy_bill_number = PN N` or `PN P-N`, and a `congress_gov_url` pointing at `https://www.congress.gov/nomination/{congress}-th-congress/{N}` (verify URL format). Bill_details still attaches because votes.bill_proposal_id FKs through it; longer-term that should become a polymorphic ref. Verify by re-running `pnpm data:votes` after the fix and confirming any new PN rolls land as `type=appointment`. <!--id:FIX-165-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`rebuild_entity_connections()` statement timeout** — First successful GitHub Actions nightly (FIX-170) surfaced PG error 57014 ("canceling statement due to statement timeout"). Migration `20260422000005` set the function-level `statement_timeout` to `120s`; the rebuild completed in 96s on 2026-04-28, then two days of vote/data growth tipped it past the cap. Bump to `15min` in [supabase/migrations/20260430000000_rebuild_entity_connections_timeout_bump.sql](supabase/migrations/20260430000000_rebuild_entity_connections_timeout_bump.sql) — generous headroom for the same TRUNCATE + full-rebuild shape. If the function ever hits 15min in the future, that's the signal to refactor to incremental rebuilds rather than keep raising the cap. Caveat: PostgREST gateway timeout may also kick in around 60–120s for large RPCs; if we see HTTP 504 after the timeout bump, the call site needs to move off `admin.rpc()` to a direct Postgres connection. <!--id:FIX-171-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Wire nightly to actually run via GitHub Actions** — Diagnosed during FIX-168/169: the Vercel cron route's docstring promised a "standalone scheduler at `packages/data/src/scheduler.ts`" would pick up triggers and call `runNightlySync()`, but that file never existed. So the Vercel cron has been firing into a void — the `cron_last_run` row in `pipeline_state` was only ever updated by someone running `pnpm data:nightly` from a laptop. (a) Add `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` running on `0 2 * * *` UTC, calling new script `pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:nightly:ci` (variant of `data:nightly` without `--env-file=.env.local` so secrets come from process env). Vercel cron is kept as a canary that confirms Vercel scheduler liveness, with docstring updated to reflect the actual architecture. (b) Fix a sibling of FIX-168: [packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts:619](packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts#L619) was inserting `data_sync_log` rows with `pipeline_name` instead of `pipeline`, swallowed by try/catch, so successful nightly completions weren't being recorded in `data_sync_log` either. **Required GitHub Secrets** (Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions): `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, `CONGRESS_API_KEY`, `OPENSTATES_API_KEY`, `COURTLISTENER_API_KEY`, `REGULATIONS_API_KEY`. The Supabase URL is hardcoded in the workflow since it's public. <!--id:FIX-170-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Move `vercel.json` into `apps/civitics/`** — The Vercel project's Root Directory is set to `apps/civitics`, so per [Vercel docs](https://vercel.com/docs/projects/project-configuration#vercel.json) `vercel.json` must live inside that directory to be discovered. With it at the repo root, the cron schedules in the file are never registered with the scheduler — which is why the 02:00 UTC nightly-sync hadn't fired (preceding FIX-168 fixed the route's correctness but not the registration). Move was a `git mv` so history follows. <!--id:FIX-169-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Nightly cron correctness: data_sync_log column + Congress key name** — Two bugs that together explain why the dashboard "Last nightly" card has been showing stale data and Congress votes/officials have been silently skipped on every run. (1) [apps/civitics/app/api/cron/nightly-sync/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/cron/nightly-sync/route.ts) inserts `data_sync_log` with column `pipeline_name`, but the actual schema column (per [supabase/migrations/0022_data_sync_log_and_graph_snapshots.sql](supabase/migrations/0022_data_sync_log_and_graph_snapshots.sql)) is `pipeline`. The error is swallowed by the route's try/catch, so the trigger row never lands and the dashboard can't show that the cron fired. (2) Every Congress pipeline file uses env var `CONGRESS_API_KEY` (also what's in `.env.local`, `.env.example`, `turbo.json`), but the nightly orchestrator [packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts) checked `CONGRESS_GOV_API_KEY` — so the key was always "missing" and both `congress_officials` and `congress_votes` got status `skipped` regardless of whether the key was set. **Vercel env-var note:** if `CONGRESS_GOV_API_KEY` (and not `CONGRESS_API_KEY`) is what's currently set in Vercel, it needs to be renamed there for tonight's prod run to actually call Congress.gov. <!--id:FIX-168-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **"PAC Money by Sector" treemap shows PAC name variations instead of sectors** — The treemap in [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts) groups by `financial_entities.industry`, but that column is populated by [packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/writer.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/writer.ts) with the FEC committee master file's `CONNECTED_ORG_NM` — a parent-org/candidate/committee name, not an industry code. Verified on prod: 896 distinct values across 1,000 rows (`'NONE'`, `'WELLS FARGO AND COMPANY'`, `'OMAR'`, `'BYRON DONALDS VICTORY FUND'` …). Clean industry tags already exist in `entity_tags` (`tag_category='industry'`, 13-value taxonomy) — that's where the AI/rule taggers write. Fix: drop `financial_entities.industry` entirely, route every reader (treemap, search subtitles, group filters, official donors, sankey fallback, snapshot/entities/search APIs, several SQL RPCs) through `entity_tags`, stop the FEC writer poisoning the column, drop the `industry_hint` from the enrichment AI prompt. Sankey already uses the right pattern as a reference. <!--id:FIX-167-->

### POST-CUTOVER (Supabase Pro, shadow→public promoted 2026-04-22)

- [x] 🟠 L — **Rewrite graph chord + treemap RPCs against polymorphic financial_relationships** — restore `chord_industry_flows()`, `treemap_officials_by_donations(integer, text, text, text)`, `get_group_sector_totals(uuid[])`, `get_crossgroup_sector_totals(uuid[], uuid[])`, `get_group_connections(uuid[], integer)`, `get_connection_counts(uuid[])`. All read donor flows; replace `donor_name`/`official_id` joins with `financial_entities` + polymorphic FK joins. Called from `/api/graph/chord`, `/api/graph/snapshot`, and several dashboard panels. <!--id:FIX-097-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Rewrite officials-breakdown + donor RPCs against polymorphic schema** — restore `get_officials_breakdown()`, `get_official_donors(uuid)`, `get_pac_donations_by_party()`, `get_officials_by_filter(text, text, text)`. Dashboard Transparency + Operations panels rely on these; the officials-detail donor tab is broken. <!--id:FIX-098-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Rewrite search_graph_entities against post-promotion schema** — search currently fails (FIX-097 self-test "entity_search_finds_warren"). New shape should query officials + agencies + financial_entities in one pass, trimmed to the columns the graph actually needs. <!--id:FIX-099-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Implement rebuild_entity_connections derivation rules** — Stage 1B function still a stub returning empty set. Derive: donation (from financial_relationships), vote_yes/vote_no (from votes), co_sponsorship, appointment (career_history), oversight, holds_position, gift_received, contract_award, lobbying. Called by nightly-sync after source pipelines; the 0 entity_connections count in `/api/claude/status` is this. <!--id:FIX-100-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Re-run deferred pipelines against Pro** — Option C shipped only congress bills+votes. Still to run against Pro: FEC bulk (donor flows), USASpending, Regulations.gov, OpenStates (state legislators + state bills), CourtListener, Legistar (4 metros), tag-rules, ai-summaries, tag-ai. Each needs its shadow→public writer rewrite similar to the one done for congress. <!--id:FIX-101-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Clean up 307 orphan proposals from early broken Pro ingest runs** — duplicates of real bills, created before the trigger-body fix (migration 20260422000001). Their sibling proposals have the bill_details row and hold the votes; these orphans clutter counts. Safe to `DELETE FROM proposals WHERE id IN (…)` after confirming zero vote FKs. <!--id:FIX-102-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Fix `a.rpc(...).catch is not a function` in officials_breakdown handler** — `/api/claude/status` reports `officials_breakdown: {error, partial: true}`. The handler is chaining `.catch()` onto a supabase-js `rpc()` call, which returns a thenable-with-error-shape, not a Promise. Replace with a try/await. <!--id:FIX-103-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Recreate proposal_trending_24h materialized view + refresh_proposal_trending()** — both were dropped in the promotion migration; recreate against public.proposals. Currently nothing on the homepage "trending" path. <!--id:FIX-104-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Default /proposals landing filter to "all"** — "open" requires status='open_comment' AND metadata->>comment_period_end > now(). Post-cutover, 989 congress-bill proposals have status='introduced' with no comment period, so the "open" default landed users on an empty page. Users can still pick "Open for comment" explicitly. <!--id:FIX-105-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Add 6-digit OTP option alongside magic link in SignInForm** — today SignInForm only offers magic link (`signInWithOtp` with `emailRedirectTo`) + OAuth. Users on mobile / cross-device flows often can't click the link in the email they receive. Add a second path: call `signInWithOtp` without `emailRedirectTo` (Supabase sends the 6-digit code instead of a link), then show a 6-input form that calls `supabase.auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: 'email' })`. Both paths use the same Supabase template — one "Email OTP" template produces either a magicLink variable or a Token variable depending on how it was requested. UX: primary button "Email me a sign-in link", secondary link "Prefer a 6-digit code?" that swaps the form. <!--id:FIX-106-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Seed the 6 missing top-20 federal agencies** — DOD, TREAS, DOS, DOL, GSA, SSA aren't in `public.agencies` on Pro, so USASpending awards from these agencies are silently skipped (acronym lookup miss during FIX-101 USASpending run). Regulations.gov insert-on-miss only catches agencies that post proposals. Either seed these six directly or add a top-agencies seed to `agencies-hierarchy`. Blocks ~30% of USASpending coverage. <!--id:FIX-107-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Fix chamber/state inference in `treemap_officials_by_donations`** — the restored RPC parses `source_ids->>'fec_id'` position 0 for chamber and 2–3 for state. False positives when an official holds an old FEC filing ID whose first char is 'S' but they're actually a House rep (Shontel Brown shows as Senate/TX in post-FIX-101 Pro data; she's an Ohio Representative). Prefer `jurisdictions.short_name` via `jurisdiction_id` + `role_title ILIKE` as the primary derivation, fall back to FEC ID parsing only when those are null. <!--id:FIX-108-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Tag financial_entities with industry** — `chord_industry_flows()` joins to `entity_tags` with `tag_category='industry'`. Post-FIX-101 (FEC + USASpending) Pro has 2,639 financial_entities but zero industry tags, so every chord flow collapses to one "Untagged" bucket (~$60M+ Rep House etc). Need a rule-based or AI industry tagger pass — `financial_entities.industry` text column is already populated (CONNECTED_ORG_NM for FEC, empty for USASpending corps; NAICS code is in `financial_relationships.metadata->>'naics_code'` for contracts). Without this the chord visualization stays unusable. <!--id:FIX-109-->
- [x] 🟡 L — **Surface contract/grant flows in graph RPCs** — FIX-101 FEC + USASpending land `type='contract'` rows in `financial_relationships` (1,480 on Pro, more once grants land) and `contract_award` edges in `entity_connections`. All existing chord/treemap/donor-breakdown RPCs filter strictly to `relationship_type = 'donation'`, so government spending is invisible in any viz. Add `chord_contract_flows()` (agency → recipient sector / NAICS) and `treemap_recipients_by_contracts()` + wire them into a dashboard "Spending" panel. <!--id:FIX-110-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Delete obsolete shadow-era pipelines after FIX-101 completes** — the following paths become dead code once the remaining FIX-101 rewrites land: `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec/index.ts` (legacy FEC API — CLAUDE.md already says "do not use"), `pac-classify/index.ts` (reads dropped `donor_name`), `financial-entities/index.ts` (reads dropped `donor_name`/`name`), `connections/shadow.ts` + `connections/delta.ts` (superseded by `rebuild_entity_connections()` SQL), `initiatives/shadow-backfill.ts` (one-time migration artifact), and the `shadowClient` helper in `packages/data/src/pipelines/utils.ts`. Remove in a dedicated cleanup commit — check `data:shadow-connections` / `data:shadow-initiatives` scripts in `package.json` too. <!--id:FIX-111-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Fix `tags/rules.ts` broken column references** — rule runs during FIX-101 quick-wins verification logged two errors: `proposals.comment_period_end does not exist` and `financial_relationships.official_id does not exist`. Proposal tags and financial-entity tags paths both crash; only the officials path currently works (503 tags upserted clean). Update the queries to the post-cutover schema — comment_period_end lives on the proposal's metadata or a different column post-cutover; financial_relationships uses polymorphic `to_id`/`to_type`. <!--id:FIX-112-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Explicitly paginate officials load in FEC bulk** — PostgREST default `max_rows=1000` silently truncates `loadOfficials()` in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts`. Pro is fine (~903 active federal officials, all fit), but local dev has 9,158 active officials (Legistar + OpenStates seed lots), so 8,158 get dropped during fuzzy-match builds — a latent correctness bug that would bite us any time the federal roster grows or we process state-level races. Use a pagination loop (range + offset) or a higher explicit limit. <!--id:FIX-113-->
- [x] ⬜ L — **Switch USASpending pipeline to award data archive (bulk download)** — current pipeline hits the paginated search API: hardcoded top-20 agencies, top 100 awards ≥ $1M, FY2024 only. USASpending publishes pre-built annual archives at `https://files.usaspending.gov/award_data_archive/` with predictable filenames (`FY2026_All_Contracts_Full_YYYYMMDD.zip`, `FY2026_All_Contracts_Delta_YYYYMMDD.zip`). Static files — no rate limits, no async polling, all agencies, all award sizes. Pattern is identical to the FEC `pas224.zip` streaming approach already in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/`. Implementation: (1) detect latest Full/Delta URL from the archive index page, (2) stream-unzip and parse CSV line-by-line filtering to agencies in our `agencies` table, (3) reuse `upsertSpendingRelationshipsBatch` writer. First run: Full file (~300MB–1GB compressed for partial FY). Subsequent runs: Delta file (much smaller). Supersedes current `data:usaspending` pipeline for contracts; FIX-114 grants fetch would also switch to `FY2026_All_Assistance_*.zip`. <!--id:FIX-118-->
- [x] ⬜ M — **Fix USASpending sub-agency attribution** — bulk pipeline only matched `awarding_agency_name`, so all Forest Service contracts landed on USDA and ICE/TSA/FEMA contracts were dropped entirely (not in agencies table). Fix: (1) seed ICE, TSA, FEMA as DHS sub-agencies via migration; (2) check `awarding_sub_agency_name` before `awarding_agency_name` in pipeline; (3) add stripped "U.S." alias keys to agency map so CSV names like "Department of Agriculture" match DB entries like "U.S. Department of Agriculture". Re-ran `--force` after fix: 841,264 matched (vs 837,417 before), 3,847 additional contracts correctly attributed. <!--id:FIX-119-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Batch the reactive `findOrCreateBillProposal` path in congress bills** — the proactive sync is now batched (FIX-101 quick win) but the reactive path in `congress/votes.ts` still calls `findOrCreateBillProposal` per novel bill (3–4 RTs each — SELECT ref + INSERT proposal + INSERT bill_details + INSERT ref). Fires only for bills first seen during vote ingestion (typically a handful per run), so impact is low. If vote pipeline runtime grows, buffer novel bills during XML parse and flush in one batched pass at the end. <!--id:FIX-115-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Tighten OpenStates people-endpoint rate limiting** — current `100ms` sleep between `/people` calls triggers 429s regularly (first call of each state, mid-chamber page turns). Each 429 costs a 30s retry stall; full 50-state run burns ~2–3 min of retry time even before the daily quota hits. Raise the inter-call sleep on the people endpoint to ~1000ms or add adaptive backoff that widens after each 429. Surfaced during the FIX-101 OpenStates Pro run (quota exhausted after 11 states partly due to cumulative retry waste). <!--id:FIX-116-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Add index on enrichment_queue(entity_type, task_type) for snapshot reads** — the seed script's `fetchQueueSnapshot()` filters by (entity_type, task_type) without a covering index; at >50k queued rows each page scan becomes O(N) and hits Pro's ~8s statement timeout partway through. Seeded the Pro queue to 125,480 / 141,772 items; the ~17k gap is items whose classification fell inside a timed-out SELECT page. Adding `CREATE INDEX enrichment_queue_type_task ON enrichment_queue(entity_type, task_type)` makes the snapshot paginate cleanly; subsequent `data:enrich-seed` runs will then close the gap idempotently. <!--id:FIX-117-->
- [x] ⬜ M — **USASpending grants fetch** — FIX-101 USASpending rewrite queries `award_type_codes: ["A", "B", "C", "D"]` (procurement contracts only). Grants use codes 02/03/04/05/11 and land in a slightly different response schema. Post-FIX-101 Pro has 1,480 contracts and 0 grants. Add a second fetch pass against the grants codes if grant-level reporting becomes a product need — same batched writer, `relationship_type='grant'`. <!--id:FIX-114-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **USASpending bulk state per-environment** — `packages/data/.usaspending-bulk-state.json` tracks `lastArchiveDate` per category but not per env. After a local run records `20260406`, a prod run reads the same file, sees the date as already-processed, and exits without writing — current workaround is `--force` on every cross-env run. Key state under `envs.{supabase-url-host}.{category}` so `127.0.0.1:54321` and `xsazcoxinpgttgquwvuf.supabase.co` track independently. Migration: legacy per-category root state migrates into whichever env is active at first read; the other env starts fresh and needs one Full run. <!--id:FIX-166-->

- [x] 🔴 S — **Donations don't render as PAC donor nodes** — `entity_connections.from_type` for donor rows is `"financial_entity"`, but `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/connections/route.ts` (`mapNodeType` switch + `financialIds` filter) and `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/snapshot/route.ts` (`financialIds` filter, also selecting `name` instead of `display_name`) both matched `"financial"`. - [x] 🔴 S — **TS connections pipeline writes legacy `from_type='financial'` for donations** — `packages/data/src/pipelines/connections/index.ts:355` still emits `from_type: "financial"` for donor rows; the SQL `rebuild_entity_connections()` and the post-FIX-154 graph API both expect `"financial_entity"`. - [x] 🟡 M — **`/api/claude/status` should detect derived-edge drift** — add a self-test that compares source row counts (`financial_relationships` by relationship_type, `votes`, `career_history`, `proposal_cosponsors`, `agencies` with `governing_body_id`) against `entity_connections` row counts per type. - [x] 🟡 S — **Pipelines should announce target DB on startup** — every `pnpm data:*` script reads `.env.local` silently. Prevents accidental cross-env writes when `.env.local` was last copied from `.env.local.prod`. <!--id:FIX-158-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **`fixes:sync` should track per-environment verification** — add a `Verified: local + prod` (or `local-only`, `prod-only`) trailer convention alongside `Fixes:`; surface it in `done.log`.
